Handlers run in registration order and each sees the text as the ones before it left it. `return false[, reason]` refuses the line: nobody receives it and the sender is told why (`chat:refused` with a code and the reason as its text, or a system line for a mod older than core chat) -- this is the real mute (warden: `chat_veto_event = "chatMessageRequest"`); the first false ends the chain. The reason names the code the player's mod shows a fixed localized line for: "muted" or "filtered" as its first word (`"muted"`, `"muted: spamming"`, `"Filtered: links are not allowed"`) gives code "muted" / "filtered" with what follows the colon as the text; any other reason, or none, gives "blocked". Returning a string replaces the text; a table `{ text = ..., scope = "global"|"local" }` replaces either (any other scope refuses the line as "blocked" rather than relay it to everyone); nothing (or true) lets the line through as it is. A rewritten line is cleaned and clamped again and reaches everyone with `edited = true`. The core knows no commands (server 1.8.0): a `/` line comes here like any other, and a resource that implements a command answers `{ handled = true }` -- the line is not relayed, the sender's mod drops it quietly (`chat:ack`), no later handler sees it, and the resource replies on its own (player:tell); a `/` line nobody handles is an ordinary chat line. With no handler the line is relayed at once, without a worker round-trip. C: register_cancellable_event receives the msg JSON as data and can refuse (nonzero, reason in the buffer) but not rewrite. The sender's own line is part of the relay: the mod draws it when it comes back.

### `queueJoinRequest`

```lua
node.on("queueJoinRequest", function(json)
  return false, "reason"  -- deny
end)
```

Handler arguments: `json (table)`  
Raw arguments (node.raw.on): `-1, -1, json`  
Return: false[, reason] refuses (the player reads reason; none: "Server full!"); a number, or { position = n }, is where the player waits  

A signed-in player is about to wait in the join queue of the full server: its ticket is redeemed and the bans checked, and it has no player id. data = { accountId, name, roles, ip, installId (nil unless the directory names the launcher's installation), position, total } -- position is where it would wait, the end. Return `false[, reason]` to refuse, or a number -- or a table with `position` -- to put it there instead (1 = first; the others shift back). Every handler runs; the first refusal and the first place win. A C verdict callback (register_cancellable_event) can refuse; it moves a player with queue_move.

### `queueAdmitRequest`

```lua
node.on("queueAdmitRequest", function(json)
  return false, "reason"  -- deny
end)
```

Handler arguments: `json (table)`  
Raw arguments (node.raw.on): `-1, -1, json`  
Return: an accountId, or { accountId = n }, names who gets the place; false lets nobody in for now; nothing: queue order  

A place is free and players with their mods ready wait for it. data = { free = <places free>, candidates = { { accountId, name, position, waitedSec }, ... } } -- the ready ones only, in queue order; one still downloading keeps its place and is never a candidate. Return the accountId of a candidate to give it the place, `false` to let nobody in for now (asked again when something changes, at most once a second), or nothing for the first candidate. A C verdict callback's nonzero is `false`.

## The relay filter

One hook that is neither an event nor a request: a question the relay asks per packet. It is named like the requests, `relayRequest`, because `false` from a handler decides something -- here, that one receiver does not get one packet.

### `relayRequest`

```lua
node.relay.filter(function(fromPid, toPid, cat, sub, gid) return false end)
```

Handler arguments: `fromPlayerId, toPlayerId, category, subtype, globalId`  
Return: false hides the packet  
C: `register_relay_filter`  
Formerly: `canRelay` (removed -- subscribing to it is refused)  

Consulted per (sender, receiver, packet category, subtype, vehicle) before a relay; return false to hide the packet from that receiver. Verdicts are cached, so call node.invalidateRelayCache when the data your hook reads has changed. Subscribed through node.on("relayRequest", fn) -- or node.relay.filter(fn) -- even though it is not a builtin; the handler keeps the raw ids, as this runs on the hot path. Visibility groups are the coarse, cached-for-you alternative.

## Wire events from clients

Not a fixed list: a resource defines its own wire events by using a name. The same `node.on` subscribes to them with `(player, data)`; the name not being a builtin is what makes it a wire event.

### `<domain>:<verb>`

```lua
node.on("chat:send", function(player, data) ... end)
player:send("chat:msg", msg)          -- a table is JSON-encoded
node.broadcast("chat:msg", msg)
```

Handler arguments: `playerId, data`  

A wire event a client sent with node.emitServer(name, data) -- data is the string the client passed, by convention JSON. Any name that is not a builtin is a wire event; the naming rule is lowercase `<domain>:<verb>` (chat:send, vehicle:trigger). The same node.on subscribes; emitClient/emitAll send in the other direction. A resource defines its own events simply by using a name. One is the core's own since server 1.6.0: chat:send `{ text, scope?, cid? }` is relayed by the core (see chatMessageRequest) and reaches chat:send handlers afterwards, unchanged, for every line that passed the rate limit -- refused and handled ones included -- so a handler that counts activity keeps working; it must not relay the line itself any more (the core drops such a copy).

## Module channel

Raw bytes on a numbered channel, for native modules and their client halves.

### `module channel`

```lua
node.modules.on(channel, function(player, data) ... end)
node.modules.send(player, channel, data)  -- "all" broadcasts
```

Handler arguments: `playerId, data`  
C: `register_module_channel`  

Inbound binary data on one u32 channel, subscribed with node.onModule(channel, fn). data is an arbitrary binary string; nothing is parsed.

## Inter-resource bus

Messages between resources on the same server.

### `resource event`

```lua
node.bus.on("name", function(sourceResource, data) ... end)
node.bus.emit("name", data)
```

Handler arguments: `sourceResourceName, data`  
C: `register_resource_event`  

A message another resource (or native module) published with node.emitResource(name, data), subscribed with node.onResource(name, fn). Delivered asynchronously on the worker, to the sender as well.

## Renamed events

The spelling on the left is gone. `node.on` and `node.off` refuse it with one error naming the name on the right, and a client may not send a wire event under it either -- an old name that silently became an ordinary wire subscription would be a handler that never runs.

| Old name | Name now |
|---|---|
| `playerConnecting` | `playerAuthenticated` |
| `playerJoin` | `playerJoined` |
| `onShutdown` | `serverShutdown` |
| `onPlayerConnectRequest` | `playerConnectRequest` |
| `onVehicleSpawnRequest` | `vehicleSpawnRequest` |
| `onVehicleEnterRequest` | `vehicleEnterRequest` |
| `onVehicleExitRequest` | `vehicleExitRequest` |
| `onVehicleCouplerRequest` | `vehicleCouplerRequest` |
| `onVehicleEditRequest` | `vehicleEditRequest` |
| `onVehiclePaintRequest` | `vehiclePaintRequest` |
| `onVehicleTriggerRequest` | `vehicleTriggerRequest` |
| `onVehicleNodeGrabRequest` | `vehicleNodeGrabRequest` |
| `canRelay` | `relayRequest` |

---

# API reference

Source: https://docs.nodemp.com/ru/plugins/api/

> The server-side plugin API: Lua node.*, node.raw, the C ABI and every event, generated from sdk/api.toml.

The API a server-side plugin uses. Three faces, one description, all documented here:

| Face | Who uses it | Reference |
|---|---|---|
| **Lua `node`** | resources -- Lua scripts in `resources/<name>/server/` | [Lua API reference (node)](/plugins/api/lua/) |
| **Lua `node.raw`** | the 1:1 mirror of the C ABI underneath `node`; the escape hatch | [Raw Lua reference (node.raw.*)](/plugins/api/raw/) |
| **C `NodeApi`** | native modules -- a `.dll`/`.so` in `modules/` | [C ABI reference (NodeApi)](/plugins/api/c/) |
| **Events** | both | [Events reference](/plugins/api/events/) |

The reference pages are GENERATED from `sdk/api.toml` by `python sdk/tools/apigen.py docs`; `apigen.py docs --check` fails when they drift from the schema, when the Lua prelude defines something the schema does not document, or the other way round. The guides are written by hand:

- [Getting started](/plugins/getting-started/) -- your first resource, in ten minutes
- [Resources](/plugins/resources/) -- folder layout, `resource.toml`, lifecycle, client files, reload
- [Events](/plugins/events/) -- the three kinds of name, objects in handlers, cancelling, the streams
- [Concurrency](/plugins/concurrency/) -- the worker thread, timers, coroutines, background jobs
- [Wire events and clients](/plugins/client-scripting/) -- talking to the client half of a resource, the `domain:verb` rule
- [Native modules](/plugins/native-modules/) -- the C ABI, versioning, language hosts
- [Recipes](/plugins/examples/) -- chat commands, permissions, teleport, persistence, HTTP, radar
- [Conventions and compatibility](/plugins/conventions/) -- naming, objects, return shapes, what is promised

## Map of the Lua API

| Namespace | Functions | About |
|---|---|---|
| [Top level: events, sending, timers, coroutines](/plugins/api/lua/#top-level-events-sending-timers-coroutines) | `on`, `off`, `send`, `broadcast`, `after`, `every`, `cancel`, `defer`, `async`, `sleep`, `wait`, `yield`, `suspend`, `await`, `job`, `log` | What every resource uses every day sits directly on `node`: subscribing to events, sending to clients, timers, and the coroutine helpers. |
| [node.players -- the roster](/plugins/api/lua/#nodeplayers--the-roster) | `get`, `all`, `count`, `ids`, `find` | Look players up and iterate them. |
| [Player objects](/plugins/api/lua/#player-objects) | `isConnected`, `isLagging`, `refresh`, `kick`, `ban`, `verify`, `spawnVehicle`, `policy`, `setStrict`, `freeze`, `teleport`, `send`, `sendModule`, `setRole`, `setGroup`, `vehicles`, `position`, `focus`, `headPose`, `camera`, `inputs`, `seat`, `unseat`, `swapWith`, `resync`, `setVehicleQueue`, `applyScheduled`, `tell` | A Player is a table with `id` and a metatable. |
| [node.vehicles -- the registry](/plugins/api/lua/#nodevehicles--the-registry) | `get`, `all`, `count`, `spawn`, `deleteAll`, `byTag`, `byTags`, `transforms`, `setQueue`, `setQueueMaxDelay`, `applyScheduled` | Look vehicles up and iterate them. |
| [Vehicle objects](/plugins/api/lua/#vehicle-objects) | `exists`, `refresh`, `delete`, `transform`, `controls`, `inputs`, `electrics`, `powertrain`, `engine`, `controllers`, `couplers`, `nodes`, `breakGroups`, `paints`, `config`, `damage`, `sync`, `occupants`, `tag`, `setTag`, `removeTag`, `lock`, `unlock`, `lockState`, `releaseLock`, `setCoupler`, `reset`, `teleport`, `trigger`, `resync`, `seat`, `setGroup` | A Vehicle is a table with `id` and a metatable, lazy like a Player: the registry record is fetched on the first field read and cached until `refresh()`. |
| [node.server -- identity, limits, time, metrics](/plugins/api/lua/#nodeserver--identity-limits-time-metrics) | `name`, `setName`, `map`, `version`, `port`, `maxPlayers`, `setMaxPlayers`, `shutdown`, `maxCars`, `setMaxCars`, `protoVersion`, `uptime`, `time`, `unixTime`, `metrics`, `memory`, `platform`, `handlerStats`, `snapshot`, `nodeGrabEnabled` | The server's own facts and knobs.. |
| [node.bans](/plugins/api/lua/#nodebans) | `add`, `remove`, `has`, `all` | Bans by IP, account or launcher installation, persisted in bans.json. |
| [node.storage -- persistent key/value](/plugins/api/lua/#nodestorage--persistent-keyvalue) | `get`, `set`, `delete` | A per-resource JSON store (storage/&lt;resource&gt;.json), flushed atomically on every write.. |
| [node.fs -- files inside the resource folder](/plugins/api/lua/#nodefs--files-inside-the-resource-folder) | `read`, `write`, `writeAsync`, `list`, `exists`, `stat`, `mkdir`, `remove`, `rename`, `copy`, `watch`, `unwatch`, `join`, `dirname`, `basename`, `extension` | Files inside the resource's own folder; paths that escape it are rejected.. |
| [node.http](/plugins/api/lua/#nodehttp) | `get`, `post`, `request`, `put`, `patch`, `delete`, `head`, `fetch` | Asynchronous HTTP. |
| [node.json](/plugins/api/lua/#nodejson) | `encode`, `flatten`, `unflatten`, `diff`, `patch`, `decode` | JSON in and out. |
| [node.crypto](/plugins/api/lua/#nodecrypto) | `sha256`, `hmac`, `randomBytes`, `randomHex` | Digests and randomness.. |
| [node.log](/plugins/api/lua/#nodelog) | `warn`, `error`, `tag`, `custom`, `raw`, `sink`, `title` | `node.log(msg, ...)` prints under the resource's tag with `string.format` arguments; the table also carries the levels and the raw forms.. |
| [node.console -- the server console's commands](/plugins/api/lua/#nodeconsole--the-server-consoles-commands) | `command`, `print` | Commands the host types into the server console. |
| [node.bus -- between resources](/plugins/api/lua/#nodebus--between-resources) | `emit`, `call`, `on`, `off` | Publish/subscribe between resources on this server (and native modules). |
| [node.modules -- the binary channel](/plugins/api/lua/#nodemodules--the-binary-channel) | `on`, `off`, `send` | Raw bytes on a numbered channel, for native modules and their client halves.. |
| [node.relay -- who sees what](/plugins/api/lua/#noderelay--who-sees-what) | `filter`, `unfilter`, `invalidate` | The packet filter and its cache. |
| [node.resources and node.config](/plugins/api/lua/#noderesources-and-nodeconfig) | `reload`, `manifest` | The resource's own manifest and settings, and reloading resources by name.. |
| [node.chat](/plugins/api/lua/#nodechat) | `say`, `tell` | Speak into the chat as the server. |

# Lua API reference (node)

Source: https://docs.nodemp.com/ru/plugins/api/lua/

> Every node.* function and Player/Vehicle method a resource can call.

Generated from `sdk/api.toml` by `sdk/tools/apigen.py docs`. Do not edit; edit the schema.

This is the API a resource is written against. It lives in `sdk/lua/prelude.lua`, which the server runs into every resource's Lua state on top of `node.raw` -- the 1:1 mirror of the C ABI ([Raw Lua reference (node.raw.*)](/plugins/api/raw/)), still there as the escape hatch. Signatures read `node.ns.name(params) -> result`; `Player`/`Vehicle` are objects (methods with `:`); a `?` marks an optional parameter or a result that can be `nil`. A `data` argument that accepts a table is JSON-encoded for you; a payload documented as a table was JSON-decoded for you.

Guides: [Getting started](/plugins/getting-started/) · [Events](/plugins/events/) · [Concurrency](/plugins/concurrency/) · [Wire events and clients](/plugins/client-scripting/) · [Recipes](/plugins/examples/) · [Conventions](/plugins/conventions/)

## Top level: events, sending, timers, coroutines

What every resource uses every day sits directly on `node`: subscribing to events, sending to clients, timers, and the coroutine helpers. Handlers receive OBJECTS -- a `Player`, a `Vehicle` -- where the raw API has ids, and decoded tables where a payload is documented as JSON.

### `node.on(name, fn)`

Raw: `node.raw.on`  

Subscribes fn to an event. The name decides what fn receives: engine events pass a `Player` or a `Vehicle` (or nothing for serverTick/serverShutdown); notifications and cancellable requests pass `(player, vehicle, payload)` with the payload decoded when it is JSON on the wire (the vehicle is left out where none is involved: playerConnectRequest, playerVerifyReported); any other name is a wire event from the client half and passes `(player, data)` with data as the string the client sent. A cancellable handler returns `false[, reason]` to deny. The [events reference](/plugins/api/events/) lists every name with its exact arguments. Several handlers per name are fine, across resources too; subscribing the same function twice replaces the first. A name an event was renamed FROM (`playerJoin`, `onPlayerConnectRequest`, `canRelay`, ...; the *Formerly* line of each event) is refused: nothing is subscribed and one error per name names the new spelling. It is not an alias -- a handler written against the old name does not run, and is told so instead of going quiet.

### `node.off(name, fn?) -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.off`  

Unsubscribes this resource's handlers for the name -- all of them, or only fn -- and returns how many were removed. fn is the function that was given to node.on, matched by identity from any context, including coroutines (node.async, pg transaction bodies). A deprecated name is mapped like in node.on, so either spelling unsubscribes what either spelling subscribed.

### `node.send(target, event, data?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.emitClient`  

Sends a wire event to one player. target is a Player or an id; data is a table (JSON-encoded for you) or a string. false when the player is not connected. `player:send` is the same call.

### `node.broadcast(event, data?, except?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.emitAll, emitOthers`  

Sends a wire event to every connected player -- or to everyone except one (a Player or an id), which is the shape of a relay: the excepted player counts as the sender for the relay filter and visibility groups. data is a table or a string.

### `node.after(ms, fn) -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.setTimeout`  

Runs fn once after ms milliseconds on the worker thread; returns a timer id for `node.cancel`.

### `node.every(ms, fn, opts?) -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.setInterval`  

Runs fn every ms milliseconds on the worker thread until cancelled; returns a timer id. By default a slow fn delays the next run and runs do not stack. With opts.precise = true (server 1.11.0) the schedule holds: when the worker was busy past one or more runs, the ones it missed follow back to back, so ten seconds of every(100) give a hundred runs. fn(missed) tells how many were missed (0 when on time). At most opts.maxBacklog (default 10, up to 1000) are caught up; beyond that the schedule starts again from now and the log says so once for that timer.

### `node.cancel(timerId)`

Raw: `node.raw.clearTimer`  

Cancels a timer from `after` or `every`. Safe for an id that already fired.

### `node.defer(fn)`

Raw: `node.raw.setImmediate`  

Runs fn after the current batch of handlers has finished -- the way to act once every other handler of the event you are in has seen it.

### `node.async(fn, ...) -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.async`  

Runs fn(...) as a cooperative coroutine on the worker and returns its task id. Inside it `node.sleep`, `node.wait`, `node.await`, `node.yield` and `node.http.fetch` suspend the coroutine while everything else keeps running.

### `node.sleep(ms)`

Raw: `node.raw.sleep`  

Suspends the current coroutine for ms milliseconds. Only inside `node.async`.

### `node.wait(msOrPredicate, intervalMs?)`

Raw: `node.raw.wait`  

A number sleeps; a function suspends the coroutine until it returns truthy, polled every intervalMs (default 50). Only inside `node.async`.

### `node.yield()`

Raw: `node.raw.yield`  

Gives the worker back for one turn inside a long loop that must run on it. Fairness, not parallelism. Only inside `node.async`.

### `node.suspend(arm) -> ...`

Raw: `node.raw.suspend`  

Suspends the current coroutine and lets somebody else decide when it continues: `arm(resume)` runs first, and whatever calls `resume(...)` later -- a handler, a timer, a module answering on the bus -- returns those values from `node.suspend`. The general form of `node.sleep` and `node.await`, and the way a callback library (the `db` module's `db.lua`, for one) offers linear code. `resume` is single-shot; call it on the worker. Only inside `node.async`.

### `node.await(workFn, args?) -> any, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.await`  

Runs a SELF-CONTAINED function (no upvalues, no `node`, JSON-serialisable args and result) on a background pool thread and suspends the coroutine until it returns; gives back the result, or nil plus an error. Only inside `node.async`.

### `node.job(workFn, args?, doneFn) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.job`  

The callback form of `node.await`: workFn runs on the pool, doneFn(result, err) on the worker. false when workFn could not be dumped.

### `node.log(msg, ...)`

Raw: `node.raw.log`  

Prints msg to the server console under this resource's name. Extra arguments are `string.format` arguments: `node.log("%s joined", player.name)`. The table also carries the levels: `node.log.warn`, `node.log.error`, and the raw forms below.

### `node.config : table`

The `[config]` table of this resource's `resource.toml`, as written (strings, numbers, booleans, arrays, nested tables); an empty table when there is none. Read once at load; `node.resources.manifest().config` re-reads the file.

### `node.raw : table`

The 1:1 mirror of the C ABI: one function per C entry, ids in, tables out ([Raw Lua reference (node.raw.*)](/plugins/api/raw/)). Everything above is built on it; reach for it when you need exactly the C behaviour.

## node.players -- the roster

Look players up and iterate them. `get` returns a Player object for any id (cheap: nothing is fetched until a field is read), so it is fine to call in a 60 Hz handler.

### `node.players.get(id) -> Player?`

The Player with this id -- a Player or a number-like table. Cheap: nothing is fetched until a field is read, so it is fine inside a 60 Hz handler. nil for a negative or non-numeric id; an unknown id gives a Player whose `isConnected()` is false.

### `node.players.all() -> array<Player>`

Raw: `node.raw.getPlayers`  

Every connected player, as Players. Ascending by id.

### `node.players.count() -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.getPlayerCount`  

How many players are connected.

### `node.players.ids() -> array<number>`

Raw: `node.raw.getPlayers`  

The connected player ids.

### `node.players.find(name) -> Player?`

The connected player with this display name, case-insensitively; nil when none.

## Player objects

A Player is a table with `id` and a metatable. Reading any other field fetches the player's record once and caches it on the object (`refresh()` drops the cache); methods act by id, so an object outlives the record it was made from -- `player:isConnected()` tells you whether it still refers to someone. Two Players are `==` when their ids match; `tostring(player)` is `Player#<id> <name>`.

### `player.id : number`

The player id -- small, assigned at connect and REUSED after a disconnect. Key durable data by `name` or `ip`, not by id.

### `player.name : string?`

The display name. Still known inside a `playerLeft` handler (the prelude remembers it), nil for an id that was never seen.

### `player.ip : string?`

The remote IP as text; what an IP ban keys on. For a verified account, `accountId` is the better key -- it does not change with the player's network.

### `player.port : number?`

The remote TCP port.

### `player.connectedSeconds : number?`

Seconds since the connection was accepted.

### `player.synced : boolean?`

true once the player finished joining (received the world).

### `player.udpConnected : boolean?`

true while the UDP channel is bound; false means positions fall back to TCP.

### `player.pingSeconds : number?`

Seconds since the last keepalive: a freshness metric (0-1 healthy), not a round-trip time.

### `player.lagging : boolean?`

true between playerLagging and playerRecovered: nothing has arrived from the player for [network] lag_grace_sec (server 1.6.0). A field of the cached record; `player:isLagging()` asks the server now.

### `player.silentSeconds : number?`

Seconds since the last packet that ARRIVED from the player (any TCP frame, or a datagram from its bound UDP endpoint). Unlike pingSeconds it never counts what the server sent (server 1.6.0).

### `player.role : string?`

The role a plugin assigned with `setRole` ("" when none). Per session.

### `player.group : number?`

The visibility group; 0 is the shared world.

### `player.vehicle : Vehicle?`

The vehicle the player occupies in any seat, or nil on foot. `vehicleId` is the same as a number (-1 on foot).

### `player.seatRole : string?`

"driver", "passenger" or "none".

### `player.vehicleCount : number?`

How many vehicles the player drives or spawned.

### `player.unicycle : number?`

The global id of the walking avatar, -1 when seated.

### `player.verified : boolean?`

true when the join ticket was redeemed with the directory (wire v17): the name is then the account's username or the guest name the directory minted, and somebody other than the player vouched for it. false on a server that is not listed, for a join that brought no ticket, and for one admitted through [listing] admit_when_down.

### `player.guest : boolean?`

true when there is no account behind the name: a Test Drive session, or nothing verified at all. `verified and not guest` is "a signed-in account".

### `player.locale : string?`

The game's language as the player's mod states it ("ru_RU", "en_US", ...), so a resource can answer in it. "" until the mod's hello arrives -- it does before playerJoined -- and from a mod older than 1.7.4.

### `player.overCapacity : boolean?`

true when the player joined a full server: playerConnectRequest decides with it, and only an explicit `true` from a handler lets such a player in (a reserved slot). Stays true for the session.

### `player.frozen : boolean?`

true while a resource holds the player frozen (Player:freeze).

### `player.accountId : number?`

The directory's account id, nil for a guest. Stable across sessions and addresses -- the key to persist anything about a player by, and what `node.bans.add(accountId)` bans.

### `player.accountRoles : string?`

The directory's role string for the account ("ADM" for a directory admin; "" for everyone else and for guests). Distinct from `role`, which is this server's own per-session role.

### `player.identifiers : array<string>?`

What the directory knows the player by: "nodemp:&lt;id&gt;", "discord:&lt;id&gt;", "ip:&lt;addr&gt;". Empty for an unverified player.

### `player.installId : string?`

The player's launcher installation as the directory names it to this server: 64 hex digits, the same for a new account from the same launcher, different on every other server, and new for every player when the directory rotates its key. nil when the launcher sent none or nothing was verified. Other players never receive it. What `node.bans.add(installId)` bans.

### `player:isConnected() -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.isPlayerConnected`  

true while a player with this id is connected. Ids are reused, so this cannot say it is the SAME player you saw earlier.

### `player:isLagging() -> boolean`

true while the player is lagging: silent past [network] lag_grace_sec, between playerLagging and playerRecovered (server 1.6.0). Asks the server now, not the cached record. false for a player that is not connected. A lagging player keeps its seat and cars (see playerLagging for when that changes).

### `player:refresh() -> Player`

Drops the cached record so the next field read fetches it again. Returns the player for chaining.

### `player:kick(reason?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.kickPlayer`  

Disconnects the player, showing the reason. false for an unknown id.

### `player:ban(reason?, untilUnix?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.banPlayer`  

Bans the player and kicks it with the reason: its account when one was verified (the ban follows the account to any address), its launcher installation when the directory named one (a new account from it is kept out too) and its IP always. Persisted in bans.json. With untilUnix (unix seconds, e.g. `os.time() + 2 * 3600`) the ban ends then and the server drops it by itself (server 1.8.0); without, it is for good.

### `player:verify(level) -> boolean?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.verifyPlayer`  

Asks the player's launcher helper to check the game install again, now, at level `"size"` (file lengths), `"scripts"` (plus a hash of the game's lua/ and ui/), `"full"` (hash everything -- slow, an audit) or `"strict"` (the archive tables of contents against the server's reference manifest, the whole game root, the user folder). The answer arrives as the `playerVerifyReported` event, after the server applied its own verdict (a "differs" or "failed" report kicks the player when [integrity] verify is not off). Returns true when the request went out, or nil and a reason: `"unknown player"`; `"unsupported"` -- that level is not available for this player (its launcher is too old for it, or strict is not on offer because this server has no reference manifest); `"pending"` -- a request to this player is still counted as in flight (the server accepts one per player per 60 s; the check every join runs does not count, so a `player:verify` from a `playerJoined` handler goes out at once). Any other level raises `Player:verify: unknown level '...'` at the caller's line.

### `player:spawnVehicle(config, transform?) -> Vehicle?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.spawnVehicleForPlayer`  

Spawns a car for the player from a server-supplied config and seats them in it: `config` is a table or JSON in the client spawn schema (`{ jbm = "...", vcf = { parts = {...}, vars = {...}, paints = {...} } }`), `transform = { pos = { x, y, z }, rot = { x, y, z, w }? }` (no `rot` = identity; no transform = whatever `pos`/`rot` the config carries). The player becomes spawner, driver and sync authority; the position goes into the cached config so a late joiner sees the car where it is. `cars_per_player` applies as for the player's own spawn; a car the player was driving is left first; the player's walking avatar is deleted like for any seat. `vehicleSpawned` fires; `vehicleSpawnRequest` is not asked -- the server does not veto itself. Returns the `Vehicle`, or `nil, "not_joined" | "max_cars" | "bad_config"` (unreadable config, no `jbm`, a unicycle, or a bad transform).

### `player:policy() -> record{lockMode,allowTrigger,allowGrab}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getPlayerPolicy`  

The player's own vehicle policy from the mod's settings (Session::Policy, wire v21): `lockMode` ("unlocked", "locked", "driver-only") is the lock the server puts on every car this player spawns, `allowTrigger` / `allowGrab` whether others may use its cars' triggers / grab their nodes. nil until the client has sent one. `playerPolicyChanged` fires on every change.

### `player:setStrict(on) -> boolean?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.setPlayerStrict`  

Marks the session strict (`on` true) or lifts the mark (`false` or `nil`; any value other than `false`/`nil` counts as on -- including `0`). The server then refuses Vehicle::Camera frames FROM the player that target a vehicle it does not occupy -- they are not relayed, so a strict client cannot follow other people's cars with its camera; frames from other players to it are unaffected. A refused report still fires `playerCameraChanged`, with `denied = true` in `player:camera()`. Server-side only: tell the client about its strict mode yourself (the session config). Per session, cleared at disconnect. Returns true, or nil and `"unknown player"`.

### `player:freeze(on) -> boolean?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.freezePlayer`  

Freezes the player (`on` true) or thaws it (`false` or `nil`), server 1.8.0. The server enforces it whatever the client does: its cars hold where they stand for everyone else (their positions and inputs are not relayed), and its spawns, rebuilds, resets and seat changes are refused with "You are frozen by the server". The player's mod (1.7.4 and later) also freezes the car under it and blocks the keys that would undo that. `playerFrozenChanged` fires on a change; `player.frozen` reads it. Per session: a player who rejoins comes back thawed -- freeze again from playerJoined if you keep a list. Returns true, or nil and `"unknown player"`.

### `player:teleport(transform) -> boolean?, string?`

Moves what the player is in -- the car it drives, or its walking avatar -- to `transform = {pos = {x, y, z}, rot = {x, y, z, w}?}` without repairing anything (Vehicle:teleport), server 1.8.0. nil and a reason when it cannot: `"not_driver"` for a passenger (move the car instead), `"no_avatar"` when the player has neither a car nor an avatar yet (try again on vehicleSpawned), `"bad_transform"`, `"unknown player"`.

### `player:send(event, data?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.emitClient`  

Sends a wire event to this player; data is a table (JSON-encoded) or a string.

### `player:sendModule(channel, data) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.sendModule`  

Sends raw bytes to this player on a u32 module channel.

### `player:setRole(role) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.setPlayerRole`  

Assigns the per-session role string ("" clears). The client shows it as a tag beside the name.

### `player:setGroup(group)`

Raw: `node.raw.setPlayerGroup`  

Puts the player in a visibility group; 0 is the shared world. Move its vehicles too (`vehicle:setGroup`).

### `player:vehicles() -> array<Vehicle>`

Raw: `node.raw.getPlayerVehicles`  

The vehicles the player drives or spawned, as Vehicles.

### `player:position() -> record{x,y,z}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getPlayerPosition`  

The last known position, from the occupied vehicle or the walking avatar; nil before the first snapshot.

### `player:focus() -> record{pos,ageSeconds}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getPlayerFocus`  

The point the relay's distance filter uses for this player: the camera when a head pose streams, else the driven vehicle.

### `player:headPose() -> record{pos,rot,freeCamera,ageSeconds}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getPlayerHeadPose`  

The last camera/head pose (about 10 Hz); nil until one arrived.

### `player:camera() -> record{vehicle,ageSeconds,denied?}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getPlayerCamera`  

Which vehicle the camera is on -- its own or the one spectated; `vehicle` is -1 for none. In a strict session (`setStrict`) a report the server refused to relay -- its target is a vehicle the player does not occupy -- carries `denied = true`; the field is absent otherwise.

### `player:inputs() -> record{vehicle,controls,controlsAgeSeconds,axes,axesAgeSeconds}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getPlayerInputs`  

Everything the player is pressing: the driven vehicle, the five controls and gear from the 60 Hz snapshot, the extra axes, each with its age. nil unless the player drives.

### `player:seat(vehicle, role?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.seatPlayer`  

Seats the player in the vehicle (a Vehicle or a gid) as "driver" (default) or "passenger", bypassing locks; a previous driver becomes a passenger.

### `player:unseat() -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.unseatPlayer`  

Puts the player on foot. false when it already is.

### `player:swapWith(other) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.swapPlayers`  

Exchanges seats -- role and vehicle -- with another Player (or id), across the whole driver/passenger/on-foot matrix.

### `player:resync() -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.resyncPlayer`  

Re-sends every vehicle's spawn and cached state to this player; returns how many bundles went out.

### `player:setVehicleQueue(mode?) -> boolean?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.setVehicleQueue`  

Scheduled changes for this player alone: `"allow"` lets other players' new cars and rebuilds wait until the player takes them (the player's own setting decides), `"off"` builds every change the moment it arrives, `nil` follows the server's mode again (`node.vehicles.setQueue`, `[vehicles] queue`). The player's mod is told at once. Per session. Returns true, or nil and `"unknown player"` / `"bad_mode"`.

### `player:applyScheduled() -> boolean?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.applyScheduledVehicles`  

The player's mod builds everything waiting for it now -- other players' new cars and part, tuning or model changes. Edits the server still holds for `[vehicles] edit_interval_sec` reach this player first, so they are built too. Returns true, or nil and `"unknown player"`.

### `player:tell(text, ...)`

Says a system line in this player's chat. Rendered by the core since server 1.6.0 (before, only by the `chat` resource, and silent without it). Extra arguments are format arguments.

## node.vehicles -- the registry

Look vehicles up and iterate them. Unicycles -- the walking avatars -- are vehicles to the server; `all()` leaves them out unless asked, `get` returns them like any other.

### `node.vehicles.get(id) -> Vehicle?`

The Vehicle with this global id, cheap like `players.get`. nil for a negative or non-numeric id; an unknown id gives a Vehicle whose `exists()` is false.

### `node.vehicles.all(includeUnicycles?) -> array<Vehicle>`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicles`  

Every vehicle in the registry as Vehicles with their records already loaded. Walking avatars (unicycles) are left out unless includeUnicycles is true.

### `node.vehicles.count() -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleCount`  

How many vehicles the registry holds, unicycles included.

### `node.vehicles.spawn(config, ownerName?) -> Vehicle?`

Raw: `node.raw.spawnVehicle`  

Spawns an ownerless vehicle from a config (a table or JSON text in the client SpawnReq schema: jbm, vcf, pos, rot, ...) and hands its simulation to the best available client. nil when nobody can host it.

### `node.vehicles.deleteAll() -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.deleteAllVehicles`  

Deletes every vehicle; returns how many.

### `node.vehicles.byTag(key, value?) -> array<Vehicle>`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehiclesByTag`  

Vehicles carrying the tag key -- any value, or exactly value.

### `node.vehicles.byTags(pairs) -> array<Vehicle>`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehiclesByTags`  

Vehicles whose tags match EVERY key = value pair of the table (AND).

### `node.vehicles.transforms(select?) -> array<record{id,pos,rot,vel,angVel,time,ping,seq,paused,transitioning,teleport,velocityStep,hasControls,controls,electrics,ageSeconds}>`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleTransformsSelect`  

The positions table: every vehicle's last snapshot in one array under one lock -- the read for a radar polling every tick. Each entry also carries controls (only when hasControls) and electrics (only when present), so full per-car state comes in one call, not a per-vehicle getter loop. select (ABI 2.4) keeps only what the caller reads: {"pos", "vel"} or {fields = {...}}, electrics = {"rpm", ...} (or true/false), controls = true/false, ids = {...}; id is always there. Without it, every key, as before.

### `node.vehicles.setQueue(mode) -> boolean?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.setVehicleQueue`  

Scheduled changes for everyone, what `[vehicles] queue` starts as: `"allow"` -- each player's mod holds other players' new cars and part, tuning or model changes, which rebuild a car and stop the game for a moment, until its player takes them; `"off"` -- every change is built as it arrives (a race, a league). A player with a mode of its own (`Player:setVehicleQueue`) keeps it. The mods are told at once. Returns true, or nil and `"bad_mode"`.

### `node.vehicles.setQueueMaxDelay(seconds) -> boolean?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.setVehicleQueueMaxDelay`  

Every mod builds what waits for it no later than this many seconds after the first of it arrived, whatever its player chose; `0` lifts the limit. What `[vehicles] queue_max_delay_sec` starts as. Returns true, or nil and `"bad_delay"` outside 0..3600.

### `node.vehicles.applyScheduled() -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.applyScheduledVehicles`  

Every mod builds everything waiting for it now -- before a race start, say. Edits the server still holds for `[vehicles] edit_interval_sec` go out first, so they are built too.

## Vehicle objects

A Vehicle is a table with `id` and a metatable, lazy like a Player: the registry record is fetched on the first field read and cached until `refresh()`. Ids that name players come as Players (`driver`, `spawner`, `authority`, `passengers`) with the raw ids beside them (`driverId`, ...). Everything that streams -- transform, controls, electrics -- is a method, because it is a fresh read each time.

### `vehicle.id : number`

The global id, unique for the life of the server.

### `vehicle.name : string?`

The owner label the spawn carried (the spawner's display name for a player's car, the ownerName given to `vehicles.spawn`).

### `vehicle.spawner : Player?`

The Player that spawned it, nil for a server-spawned one. `spawnerId` is the id (-1).

### `vehicle.driver : Player?`

The Player at the wheel, nil when empty. `driverId` is the id (-1).

### `vehicle.authority : Player?`

The Player simulating it (not necessarily the spawner). `authorityId` is the id.

### `vehicle.passengers : array<Player>`

The passengers as Players. `passengerIds` are the ids.

### `vehicle.isUnicycle : boolean?`

true for a walking avatar.

### `vehicle.idleSeconds : number?`

How long nobody has driven it; 0 while driven.

### `vehicle.spawnerOnline : boolean?`

Whether the spawning player is still connected.

### `vehicle.lockMode : string?`

"unlocked", "locked" or "driver-only". `lockWhitelist` are the player ids always allowed in.

### `vehicle.configHash : number?`

FNV-1a-32 of the cached config -- what clients echo so a desynced config is detectable.

### `vehicle.partsHash : number?`

The identity of the build: FNV-1a-32 over `jbm`, the sorted `slot=part` lines of `vcf.parts` and the sorted `name=value` lines of `vcf.vars` (numbers as `%.6g`), and nothing else -- two spawns of the same build in different places share it, a changed part changes it. `configHash` cannot say that: it covers the whole config bytes, position and rotation included. Canonical form: `&lt;jbm&gt;
<slot=part>
...
--
<name=value>
...` (ABI 2.2).

### `vehicle.damageCounter : number?`

The authority-reported damage version. `damageBlobCounter` and `damageBlobSize` describe the stored blob (0 = pristine).

### `vehicle.tags : table?`

The tags as key -> value.

### `vehicle.group : number?`

The visibility group; 0 is the shared world.

### `vehicle:exists() -> boolean`

true while the registry has this id.

### `vehicle:refresh() -> Vehicle`

Drops the cached record. Returns the vehicle for chaining.

### `vehicle:delete() -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.deleteVehicle`  

Deletes the vehicle for everyone; vehicleDeleted follows.

### `vehicle:transform() -> record{pos,rot,vel,angVel,time,ping,seq,paused,transitioning,teleport,velocityStep,hasControls,ageSeconds}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleTransform`  

The last kinematic snapshot; nil before the first. A few ticks old by construction.

### `vehicle:controls() -> record{steering,throttle,brake,clutch,parkingBrake,gear}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleControls`  

The driving inputs from the last snapshot; nil while no driver has sent one.

### `vehicle:inputs() -> record{axes,ageSeconds}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleInputs`  

The driver's extra (modded) input axes, merged from the delta stream.

### `vehicle:electrics() -> table?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleElectrics`  

The merged electrics (lights, signals, gauges).

### `vehicle:powertrain() -> table?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehiclePowertrain`  

The merged powertrain device state.

### `vehicle:engine() -> table?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleEngine`  

The merged engine state.

### `vehicle:controllers() -> table?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleControllers`  

The merged controller calls, "controller|function" -> call.

### `vehicle:couplers() -> array?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleCouplers`  

The merged coupler/door state.

### `vehicle:nodes() -> table?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleNodes`  

The last node-position (deformation) packet, as sent.

### `vehicle:breakGroups() -> array<string>?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleBreakGroups`  

The break groups reported broken.

### `vehicle:paints() -> array?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehiclePaints`  

The paint layers from the cached config.

### `vehicle:config() -> table?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleConfig`  

The cached spawn/edit config -- what a joiner receives.

### `vehicle:damage() -> record{counter,blobCounter,size,blob}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleDamage`  

Damage telemetry with the raw blob (a binary string, nil when pristine).

### `vehicle:sync() -> record{authority,authorityAgeSeconds,seatEpoch,spawnedAgeSeconds,idleSeconds,configChangedAgeSeconds,hasPos,posAgeSeconds,posSeq}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleSync`  

How alive the sync is: the authority and its heartbeat age, seat epoch, ages. The record for 'why is this car frozen'.

### `vehicle:occupants() -> array<record{player,role,seat}>`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleOccupants`  

Who is in it, driver first: `{ player = Player, role = "driver" | "passenger", seat = "FL" }` (seat "" when the car reported no layout).

### `vehicle:tag(key) -> string?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleTag`  

One tag's value.

### `vehicle:setTag(key, value) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.setVehicleTag`  

Sets a tag (key 1-64 bytes, value 1-256, 32 keys per vehicle; over-limit is rejected). Broadcast to clients; vehicleTagsChanged fires.

### `vehicle:removeTag(key) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.removeVehicleTag`  

Removes a tag; false when it was not there.

### `vehicle:lock(mode?, whitelist?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.setVehicleLock`  

Locks the vehicle against NEW seat claims: mode "locked" (default), "driver-only" or "unlocked"; whitelist is an array of Players / ids (this session's pids) and `{ account = <id> }` entries (the directory account, whoever is connected with it -- survives a reconnect), up to 128 together (ABI 2.2; was 32 pids). Nobody seated is evicted; server-driven seating bypasses locks. The lock is then the resource's: the spawner's own "who may enter my car" setting (Session::Policy) no longer changes this vehicle until `releaseLock()`.

### `vehicle:unlock() -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.setVehicleLock`  

`lock("unlocked")`.

### `vehicle:lockState() -> record{mode,whitelist,owner}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getVehicleLock`  

The current lock: `mode`, `whitelist` (the pids as given), `accounts` (the account ids as given, ABI 2.2) and `owner` -- "plugin" when a resource set it (the spawner's policy leaves it alone), "policy" otherwise.

### `vehicle:releaseLock() -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.releaseVehicleLock`  

Gives a lock this resource set back to the spawner's own setting: the spawner's lock mode with the spawner whitelisted (unlocked when the spawner is gone), broadcast at once, `vehicleLockChanged` fires. Use it when the resource stops managing the car; `unlock()` is different -- it is a plugin-owned "unlocked".

### `vehicle:setCoupler(name, open?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.setVehicleCoupler`  

Opens (default) or closes the named coupler group on every client and caches it.

### `vehicle:reset(transform?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.resetVehicle`  

Repairs the car from the server: with no argument where it stands (its last known position), or reset to `transform = {pos = {x, y, z}, rot = {x, y, z, w}?}`. What a driver's own reset does -- the damage cache is cleared, every client applies the reset, the driver included -- and `vehicleReset` fires with `player = nil` and `data.cause = "server"`. A strict session does not count it as the player's reset. false for an unknown vehicle or one that never sent a position.

### `vehicle:teleport(transform) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.teleportVehicle`  

Moves the car from the server WITHOUT repairing it (server 1.8.0): to `transform = {pos = {x, y, z}, rot = {x, y, z, w}?}`, keeping the way it faces when rot is left out. Damage, electrics and cargo stay -- the admin's /tp, a rescue from under the map. The car's own client moves it and every other client snaps it; the car's next position carries the teleport flag, so `vehicleTeleported` fires (not vehicleReset). A player whose mod is older than 1.7.4 sees the car repaired as by Vehicle:reset. false for an unknown vehicle or a bad transform.

### `vehicle:trigger(call) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.triggerVehicle`  

Has the sync authority execute one controller call -- a table or JSON `{controllerName, functionName, ...}`. Never asks vehicleTriggerRequest: the server does not veto itself.

### `vehicle:resync(to?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.resyncVehicle`  

Re-sends the spawn and cached state to one Player (or id), or to everyone.

### `vehicle:seat(player, role?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.seatPlayer`  

Seats a Player (or id) in this vehicle; `player:seat(vehicle)` is the same call.

### `vehicle:setGroup(group)`

Raw: `node.raw.setVehicleGroup`  

Puts the vehicle in a visibility group. A packet about a vehicle belongs to the vehicle's group, not its driver's.

## node.server -- identity, limits, time, metrics

The server's own facts and knobs.

### `node.server.name() -> string`

Raw: `node.raw.getServerName`  

The server's name.

### `node.server.setName(name) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.setServerName`  

Changes the reported name.

### `node.server.map() -> string`

Raw: `node.raw.getMap`  

The map, as the client-facing level path.

### `node.server.version() -> string`

Raw: `node.raw.getServerVersion`  

The server's version string.

### `node.server.port() -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.getPort`  

The port (TCP and UDP).

### `node.server.maxPlayers() -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.getMaxPlayers`  

The player limit in force.

### `node.server.setMaxPlayers(n) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.setMaxPlayers`  

Changes the player limit; enforced on the next connect, nobody is kicked.

### `node.server.shutdown(reason?, delayMs?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.shutdownServer`  

Stops the server the way Ctrl+C does (server 1.8.0): everyone is kicked with the reason (default "Server shutdown"), serverShutdown and resourceUnload("shutdown") fire, storage is written, the process exits with code 0 -- and a Docker or systemd restart policy turns that into a restart. delayMs schedules it and fires serverShutdownScheduled, the moment to announce a countdown in chat; calling again replaces the schedule, and a negative delayMs cancels it. false when the server is already stopping or there was nothing to cancel.

### `node.server.maxCars() -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.getMaxCars`  

The per-player vehicle limit in force.

### `node.server.setMaxCars(n) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.setMaxCars`  

Changes the per-player vehicle limit; enforced on the next spawn.

### `node.server.protoVersion() -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.getProtoVersion`  

The wire protocol version this server speaks (21 at ABI 2.1). The core kicks a mod whose session:hello names another version; a resource with a check of its own compares against this value, never against a literal -- a literal drifts the next time the protocol moves.

### `node.server.uptime() -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.getServerUptime`  

Seconds since start (monotonic, fractional).

### `node.server.time() -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.getTime`  

Unix time with fractions.

### `node.server.unixTime() -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.getUnixTime`  

Unix time in whole seconds.

### `node.server.metrics() -> table`

Raw: `node.raw.getMetrics`  

Live metrics: players, vehicles, uptime, net counters, connection limiter, plugin queue, and (ABI 2.3) `platform` and `memory`.

### `node.server.memory() -> table`

Raw: `node.raw.getMemory`  

`{ lua, luaAll, process }` in bytes: this resource's Lua state (what `collectgarbage("count") * 1024` says), every loaded resource's state together, and the server process's resident set (0 where the platform gives none). Which one grows after an evening of `/reload` tells a leaking cache from a leaking server (ABI 2.3).

### `node.server.platform() -> table`

Raw: `node.raw.getPlatform`  

`{ os = "windows" | "linux" | "macos", arch = "x64" | "arm64", version }` -- constant for the process. For messages that name `db.dll` or `db.so`, and for reports (ABI 2.3).

### `node.server.handlerStats(reset?) -> table`

Raw: `node.raw.getHandlerStats`  

How long this resource's handlers run, from the same clock as the stall watchdog: `{ count, min, max, mean, stddev, over, thresholdMs, byKind = { event = {...}, timer = {...}, ["async task"] = {...} } }`, milliseconds of the code's own time. `over` grows exactly when the log says `stalled the thread`; `mean` and `max` show a handler creeping toward the 250 ms threshold before it does. `reset = true` zeroes the numbers after reading. Another resource sees its own numbers, not these (ABI 2.3).

### `node.server.snapshot() -> record{time,players,vehicles}`

Raw: `node.raw.getWorldSnapshot`  

One bulk read of the world: players with position and seat, vehicles with pos/rot/vel.

### `node.server.nodeGrabEnabled() -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.isNodeGrabEnabled`  

Whether the experimental node grabber is enabled in server.toml.

## node.bans

Bans by IP, account or launcher installation, persisted in bans.json. `Player:ban()` is the one that also kicks.

### `node.bans.add(who, reason?, untilUnix?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.banIp`  

Bans for future connects. `who` is an IP string (banIp), a directory account id number (banAccount, wire v17: the ban follows the account to any address), a launcher installation id -- a string of 64 hex digits, as `player.installId` gives it (banInstallId: every account from that launcher, Test Drive included; the id is this server's alone and changes when the directory rotates its key) -- or a Player (banPlayer: account when verified, installation when named, IP always, and the player is kicked). Persisted in bans.json. An existing session stays for the first three forms. untilUnix (unix seconds, server 1.8.0) makes it a timed ban: it ends then, and the server drops it from bans.json by itself -- no timer of yours, nothing lost on a restart.

### `node.bans.remove(who) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.unbanIp`  

Lifts a ban: an IP string, an account id number, or a launcher installation id. False when it was not banned.

### `node.bans.has(who) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.isBanned`  

Whether an IP string, an account id number, a launcher installation id, or a Player (its account, its installation or its IP) is banned.

### `node.bans.all() -> array<record{ip,account,installId,reason,at,name,until}>`

Raw: `node.raw.getBans`  

Every ban: IP bans carry ip, account bans carry account (the directory's id), launcher installation bans carry installId; a timed ban carries until (unix seconds).

## node.storage -- persistent key/value

A per-resource JSON store (storage/&lt;resource&gt;.json), flushed atomically on every write.

### `node.storage.get(key, default?) -> any`

Raw: `node.raw.storageGet`  

The stored value, or default when the key is absent.

### `node.storage.set(key, value) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.storageSet`  

Stores any JSON-serialisable value; flushed atomically.

### `node.storage.delete(key) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.storageDelete`  

Removes a key; false when absent.

## node.fs -- files inside the resource folder

Files inside the resource's own folder; paths that escape it are rejected.

### `node.fs.read(path) -> string?`

Raw: `node.raw.readFile`  

Reads a file inside the resource folder; nil when missing or outside it.

### `node.fs.write(path, data) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.writeFile`  

Writes a file inside the resource folder, creating folders. Synchronous.

### `node.fs.writeAsync(path, data, cb?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.writeFileAsync`  

Writes off the worker; cb(ok) runs on the worker when done. true means accepted.

### `node.fs.list(path?) -> array<record{name,dir,size}>?`

Raw: `node.raw.listFiles`  

Lists a folder inside the resource folder (the root when omitted).

### `node.fs.exists(path) -> string?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.fileExists`  

`"file"`, `"dir"` or `nil` -- a stat, the file is not read. `nil, "outside"` for an absolute path or a `..` step, which `read()` could never tell from a missing file (ABI 2.3).

### `node.fs.stat(path) -> table?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.fileStat`  

`{ dir, size, mtime }` -- `mtime` in unix seconds with a fraction, like `node.server.time()`; `nil, err` for `"outside"` or `"not found"` (ABI 2.3).

### `node.fs.mkdir(path) -> boolean, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.makeDir`  

Creates the folder and its missing parents (`mkdir("a/b/c")`); true when it already exists (ABI 2.3).

### `node.fs.remove(path, recursive?) -> boolean, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.removeFile`  

Removes a file or an empty folder; a folder with contents needs `recursive = true` (`false, "not empty"` otherwise). Never the resource folder itself (ABI 2.3).

### `node.fs.rename(from, to) -> boolean, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.renameFile`  

Renames or moves inside the resource folder, replacing an existing target file -- atomically where the OS does, so `write(tmp)` then `rename(tmp, target)` is the atomic write `write()` itself is not (ABI 2.3).

### `node.fs.copy(from, to) -> boolean, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.copyFile`  

Copies a file, replacing the target; missing parents are created (ABI 2.3).

### `node.fs.watch(path, fn) -> number?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.watchFile`  

`fn(path, "changed" | "removed")` within about a second of the file changing, appearing or going -- the modification time is polled once a second off the worker, so it works the same on every OS. Returns an id for `unwatch`, or `nil, "limit"` past 64 watches per resource. Watches go with the resource at unload (ABI 2.3).

### `node.fs.unwatch(id) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.unwatchFile`  

Stops a watch of this resource's (ABI 2.3).

### `node.fs.join(...) -> string`

Joins path segments with `/`, collapsing doubled separators: `join("i18n", "ru.json")` is `"i18n/ru.json"`. Pure Lua, no sandbox check -- the function that takes the result does that (ABI 2.3).

### `node.fs.dirname(path) -> string`

Everything before the last `/` (`""` for a bare name). Pure Lua (ABI 2.3).

### `node.fs.basename(path) -> string`

Everything after the last `/`. Pure Lua (ABI 2.3).

### `node.fs.extension(path) -> string`

The last `.suffix` of the base name, dot included (`".json"`), or `""`. Pure Lua (ABI 2.3).

## node.http

Asynchronous HTTP. Callbacks run on the worker; `fetch` is the coroutine form for use inside `node.async`.

### `node.http.get(url, headers?, cb) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.httpGet`  

GET; cb(status, body, headers) on the worker: status is the HTTP status code, or -1 with the error text in body when the request failed (could not resolve or connect, TLS handshake or certificate verification, timeout, malformed or oversized response). Returns false when the request could not be queued -- cb then never runs. About 15 s timeout, 8 MB body. The peer certificate is verified (against `[http] ca_file` when the hoster names one) unless `[http] insecure` is set; a failure is a -1.

### `node.http.post(url, body, headers?, cb) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.httpPost`  

POST; body is a table (JSON-encoded) or a string. cb and the return value are as for `get`: status -1 with the error text in body when the request failed, false when it could not be queued.

### `node.http.request(method, url, opts?, cb) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.httpRequest`  

Any method (server 1.2.0): `node.http.request("PUT", url, { headers = h, body = t }, cb)`. method is a token of letters A-Z only, at most 16 -- `"GET"`, `"POST"`, `"PUT"`, `"PATCH"`, `"DELETE"`, `"HEAD"` or a custom one the service understands; the prelude upper-cases it for you, anything else comes back as status -1 with body "invalid HTTP method"; opts.headers is a table of header -> value, opts.body a table (JSON-encoded) or a string, both optional (opts itself may be nil). cb(status, body, headers) and the return value are exactly as for `get`: the HTTP status code, or -1 with the error text in body when the request failed; false when the request could not be queued (cb then never runs). A HEAD answer carries status and headers and an empty body. `put`, `patch`, `delete` and `head` are this with the method fixed.

### `node.http.put(url, body?, headers?, cb) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.httpRequest`  

PUT; body is a table (JSON-encoded), a string, or nil for none. cb and the return value are as for `get`.

### `node.http.patch(url, body?, headers?, cb) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.httpRequest`  

PATCH; body is a table (JSON-encoded), a string, or nil for none. cb and the return value are as for `get`.

### `node.http.delete(url, body?, headers?, cb) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.httpRequest`  

DELETE; the body is optional (most services want none: `node.http.delete(url, nil, headers, cb)` or `node.http.delete(url, cb)`). cb and the return value are as for `get`.

### `node.http.head(url, headers?, cb) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.httpRequest`  

HEAD; cb(status, "", headers) -- the status and the response headers, never a body. The return value is as for `get`.

### `node.http.fetch(url, opts?) -> number, string, table`

The coroutine form, inside `node.async` only: `local status, body, headers = node.http.fetch(url, { method = "DELETE", body = t, headers = h })`. opts.method is any method `request` accepts (default GET; before 1.2.0 anything but POST was sent as GET). status is the HTTP status code, or -1 with the error text in body when the request failed; it is 0, with body "request not queued", only when the request could not be queued at all (the background pool is full or shutting down).

## node.json

JSON in and out. Most of the API encodes for you (a table given to `send`, `broadcast`, `bus.emit`, `storage.set` is encoded); these are for everything else.

### `node.json.encode(value, opts?) -> string`

Raw: `node.raw.jsonEncode`  

Encodes a Lua value as JSON. `opts = { pretty = true }` indents by two spaces, `{ pretty = 4 }` by four (ABI 2.3); without `opts` the compact form, byte for byte as before.

### `node.json.flatten(value) -> table`

Raw: `node.raw.jsonFlatten`  

The value as one flat table of RFC 6901 JSON-pointer keys: `{ ["/inventory/slots/3/item"] = "wrench" }`; a scalar becomes `{ [""] = value }`. An empty table has no leaves and is dropped -- `unflatten` cannot bring it back (ABI 2.3).

### `node.json.unflatten(flat) -> any?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.jsonUnflatten`  

The nested value back from a flat table of JSON-pointer keys; `nil, err` for a key that is not a pointer, conflicting keys, or a result too deep (ABI 2.3).

### `node.json.diff(a, b) -> array`

Raw: `node.raw.jsonDiff`  

RFC 6902 operations that turn `a` into `b`: an array of `{ op = "add" | "remove" | "replace", path, value? }`, empty for equal values. `json.patch(a, json.diff(a, b))` is `b` up to the Lua-to-JSON conversion (ABI 2.3).

### `node.json.patch(base, ops) -> any?, string?`

Raw: `node.raw.jsonPatch`  

Applies RFC 6902 operations (`add`, `remove`, `replace`, `move`, `copy`, `test`) to a copy of `base`; `base` is untouched. `nil, err` on a missing path, a failed `test`, an unknown op, or a result too deep (ABI 2.3).

### `node.json.decode(text) -> any?`

Raw: `node.raw.jsonDecode`  

Decodes JSON text; nil on a parse error.

## node.crypto

Digests and randomness.

### `node.crypto.sha256(data) -> string`

Raw: `node.raw.sha256`  

SHA-256 as 64 hex characters.

### `node.crypto.hmac(key, data) -> string`

Raw: `node.raw.hmac`  

HMAC-SHA256 as hex.

### `node.crypto.randomBytes(n) -> string?`

Raw: `node.raw.randomBytes`  

n cryptographically random bytes as a binary string (1..65536).

### `node.crypto.randomHex(n?) -> string?`

n random bytes (default 16) as hex -- a token.

## node.log

`node.log(msg, ...)` prints under the resource's tag with `string.format` arguments; the table also carries the levels and the raw forms.

### `node.log.warn(msg, ...)`

Raw: `node.raw.logWarn`  

Logs at the warning level under this resource's name; format arguments accepted.

### `node.log.error(msg, ...)`

Raw: `node.raw.logError`  

Logs at the error level under this resource's name.

### `node.log.tag(tag, msg, ...)`

Raw: `node.raw.logTag`  

Logs under one of the server's own tags (Core, Net, Res, Mods, Module, Join, Leave, Kick, Veh, Warn, Error, Debug).

### `node.log.custom(tag, rgb, msg, ...)`

Raw: `node.raw.logCustom`  

Logs under a tag of your own in a 0xRRGGBB colour.

### `node.log.raw(text)`

Raw: `node.raw.logRaw`  

Writes text to the console as-is, no timestamp or tag.

### `node.log.sink(fn)`

Raw: `node.raw.setLogSink`  

Observes every server log line as fn(tag, level, msg) on the worker; nil clears. Cannot suppress lines.

### `node.log.title(title)`

Raw: `node.raw.setConsoleTitle`  

Sets the console window title.

## node.console -- the server console's commands

Commands the host types into the server console. A line no built-in command takes reaches `consoleInput`; `command` is the convenient form, and `help` lists what resources added.

### `node.console.command(name, fn, help?)`

Adds a server console command (server 1.11.0): a line whose first word is name, in any case, calls fn(args, line) -- args is the rest of the line, trimmed -- and what fn returns is printed (a string or an array of strings; nil prints nothing). help is one line for the console's help list, which shows the resource's name beside it. Built-in commands (help, status, players, kick, say, reload, stop) win over a resource's of the same name. The same name again replaces the earlier fn. Built on consoleInput.

### `node.console.print(text)`

Raw: `node.raw.logRaw`  

Prints text on the console as it is, without a time or a tag -- the same as node.log.raw.

## node.bus -- between resources

Publish/subscribe between resources on this server (and native modules). Asynchronous, delivered to the sender too.

### `node.bus.emit(name, data?)`

Raw: `node.raw.emitResource`  

Publishes to every resource and native module subscribed to name (the sender included), asynchronously on the worker. data is a table (JSON-encoded) or a string.

### `node.bus.call(name, data?) -> array<record{source,value}>`

Raw: `node.raw.callResource`  

Asks every resource subscribed to name -- the caller included -- and returns what their handlers return, as an array of { source, value } in subscription order: a handler that returns nil is left out, a table answer arrives as a table. The handlers run now, before call returns, so an answer is what the other resource knows at this moment; a handler that has to wait for something answers later with emit. data is a table (JSON-encoded) or a string. A native module subscribed to name sees the call and cannot answer. Raises when calls nest deeper than 8 (a handler that calls back into the asker).

### `node.bus.on(name, fn)`

Raw: `node.raw.onResource`  

Subscribes fn(sourceResourceName, data) to bus messages under name; a native module appears as "native". What fn returns is its answer when the message came from node.bus.call, and is ignored after emit.

### `node.bus.off(name, fn?) -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.offResource`  

Unsubscribes this resource's bus handlers for name -- all of them, or only fn, matched by identity from any context, including coroutines.

## node.modules -- the binary channel

Raw bytes on a numbered channel, for native modules and their client halves.

### `node.modules.on(channel, fn)`

Raw: `node.raw.onModule`  

Subscribes fn(player, data) to raw bytes clients send on a u32 channel.

### `node.modules.off(channel, fn?) -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.offModule`  

Unsubscribes this resource's handlers for the channel -- all of them, or only fn, matched by identity from any context, including coroutines.

### `node.modules.send(target, channel, data) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.sendModule`  

Sends raw bytes to a Player (or id), or to everyone with target "all" or nil.

## node.relay -- who sees what

The packet filter and its cache. Visibility groups (`Player:setGroup`, `Vehicle:setGroup`) are the cheap alternative for room-style rules.

### `node.relay.filter(fn)`

Raw: `node.raw.on`  

Installs a relayRequest hook (the same thing as `node.on("relayRequest", fn)`): fn(fromPid, toPid, category, subtype, globalId) returns false to hide a packet from that recipient. Runs with ids on the hot path; verdicts are cached until `invalidate`. Prefer visibility groups for room-style rules.

### `node.relay.unfilter(fn?) -> number`

Raw: `node.raw.off`  

Removes this resource's hooks, or only fn.

### `node.relay.invalidate()`

Raw: `node.raw.invalidateRelayCache`  

Drops every cached verdict so the hooks are consulted again. Call it whenever the data a hook reads has changed.

## node.resources and node.config

The resource's own manifest and settings, and reloading resources by name.

### `node.resources.reload(name) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.reloadResource`  

Queues a reload of a resource by name -- registrations, timers and coroutines dropped, the folder loaded again. true means accepted. A resource may reload itself.

### `node.resources.manifest() -> record{name,version,type,server,client,config}?`

Raw: `node.raw.getManifest`  

This resource's `resource.toml` as a table, re-read from disk.

## node.chat

Speak into the chat as the server. Chat is part of the core since server 1.6.0: these lines reach the players with no resource installed. To refuse or rewrite what players say, subscribe to `chatMessageRequest`; to observe it, `chatMessage`.

### `node.chat.say(text, ...)`

Says a system line in everyone's chat (the core renders it since server 1.6.0; published on the bus as chat:say). Format arguments accepted.

### `node.chat.tell(target, text, ...)`

Says a system line in one player's chat; target is a Player or an id.

## node.queue -- the join queue

The players waiting for a place on a full server ([queue] in server.toml). A waiting player is no player yet: it is named by its directory account id. The events that decide and follow the queue -- queueJoinRequest, queueAdmitRequest, queueAdmitted, queueLeft, queueNoShow, playerJoinSlow -- are in the events reference.

### `node.queue.list() -> array<record{accountId,name,roles,ip,installId,position,ready,admitWhenReady,waitedSec}>`

Raw: `node.raw.getQueue`  

Who waits in the join queue, first first: { accountId, name, roles, ip, installId, position, ready, admitWhenReady, waitedSec } each. ready means the mods are in the player's cache; a free place goes to the first ready one.

### `node.queue.move(accountId, position) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.queueMove`  

Moves a waiting account to position (1 = first; past the end = last). false when it is not waiting.

### `node.queue.first(accountId) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.queueMove`  

Puts a waiting account first. false when it is not waiting.

### `node.queue.remove(accountId, reason?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.queueRemove`  

Takes a waiting account out of the queue; the player reads reason (default "Removed from the join queue"). false when it is not waiting.

### `node.queue.admit(accountId) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.queueAdmit`  

Lets a waiting account in past max_players as soon as its mods are ready. false when it is not waiting.

### `node.queue.pause(note?) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.queuePause`  

Lets nobody in from the queue until resume(); every waiting player reads note under its position. Free places stay free, newcomers still queue, downloads go on.

### `node.queue.resume() -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.queuePause`  

Lets the queue go on after pause().

### `node.queue.paused() -> boolean, string`

Raw: `node.raw.getQueue`  

Whether the queue is paused, and the note the waiting players read.

### `node.queue.limits() -> record{enabled,maxWaiting,maxWaitingPerIp,downloadsWhileWaiting,maxNoShows,noShowWindowMin,noShowBarMin}`

Raw: `node.raw.getQueueLimits`  

The [queue] settings as they are now.

### `node.queue.setLimits(limits) -> boolean`

Raw: `node.raw.setQueueLimits`  

Changes [queue] settings at runtime: a table with any of the keys limits() returns. They apply to the next decision and never remove anyone who already waits; not written to server.toml. false, and nothing changed, when a value is out of range.

---

# Raw Lua reference (node.raw.*)

Source: https://docs.nodemp.com/ru/plugins/api/raw/

> node.raw: the 1:1 Lua mirror of the C ABI.

Generated from `sdk/api.toml` by `sdk/tools/apigen.py docs`. Do not edit; edit the schema.

`node.raw` is the 1:1 mirror of the C ABI inside a resource's Lua state -- one function per C entry, ids in, tables out. The API a resource is written against is [Lua API reference (node)](/plugins/api/lua/); this page is the layer underneath it, kept reachable as the escape hatch and as the map between a Lua name and its C entry. Signatures read `node.name(params) -> result`; a `?` marks an optional parameter or a result that can be `nil`. Result shapes are MEASURED against a live server (`array<...>`, `record{keys}`), not declared. Where a description shared with the C reference says "returns 0 on success" or "-1 when", the Lua binding returns the shape shown in the signature instead: `boolean` for a status, `nil` for a miss. Functions marked *Lua only* have no C counterpart.
