## Console and logging

Writing to the server console. Every line is tagged; a resource's plain node.log carries the resource's own name as the tag, node.logTag uses one of the server's known tags, node.logCustom a tag of your own in a chosen colour. A log sink lets a resource observe every line the server prints (asynchronously, on the plugin worker).

### `node.raw.log(msg) -> nil`

Arguments: `msg: string`  
C: `log_info`

Prints msg to the server console under this resource's own tag, at the info level. node.logWarn and node.logError are the two other levels; node.logTag logs under one of the server's tags instead of yours.

### `node.raw.logWarn(msg) -> nil`

Arguments: `msg: string`  
C: `log_warn`

Logs msg under this resource's tag at the warning level (the console's Warn colour). Same as node.logTag("Warn", msg) but keeps your resource as the tag.

### `node.raw.logError(msg) -> nil`

Arguments: `msg: string`  
C: `log_error`

Logs msg under this resource's tag at the error level (the console's Error colour). Same as node.logTag("Error", msg) but keeps your resource as the tag.

### `node.raw.logTag(tag, msg) -> nil`

Arguments: `tag: string`, `msg: string`  
C: `log_tag`

Logs msg under one of the server's own console tags -- Core, Net, Res, Mods, Module, Join, Leave, Kick, Veh, Warn, Error, Debug (case-insensitive) -- in the house format `HH:MM:SS Tag > msg` with that tag's colour. An unknown tag falls back to Module. Use "Warn" and "Error" for the coloured levels, since node.log itself logs at info.

### `node.raw.logCustom(tag, rgb, msg) -> nil`

Arguments: `tag: string`, `rgb: number`, `msg: string`  
C: `log_custom`

Logs msg under a tag of your own in a chosen colour. rgb is 0xRRGGBB (0xFF8800); the server maps it to the nearest ANSI-256 colour, so the exact shade depends on the terminal. Keep the tag at six characters or fewer to preserve the column alignment.

### `node.raw.logRaw(text) -> nil`

Arguments: `text: string`  
C: `log_raw`

Writes text to the console as-is: no timestamp, no tag, one line per call. It may contain ANSI escapes; the copy written to logs/server.log has them stripped.

### `node.raw.setLogSink(fn) -> nil`

Arguments: `fn: any`  
C: `set_log_sink`

Observes every line the server logs as fn(tag, level, msg), asynchronously on the worker thread. Unlike the C set_log_sink it cannot suppress a line (Lua never runs on the logging thread). One sink per resource; nil clears it.

### `node.raw.setConsoleTitle(title) -> nil`

Arguments: `title: string`  
C: `set_console_title`

Sets the console window title (SetConsoleTitle on Windows, an OSC escape elsewhere when the terminal supports ANSI; otherwise nothing happens).

## Server: info, settings, time and metrics

Read the server's identity (name, map, version, port, limits), change the runtime limits and the name, read time, and pull the metrics snapshot. Limit changes apply to the next check -- a connecting player, a spawn request -- and never kick anyone retroactively.

### `node.raw.getServerName() -> string`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_server_name`  
Note: [server] name

The server's name ([server] name in server.toml, or whatever setServerName set last).

### `node.raw.getMap() -> string`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_map`  
Note: [server] map

The map the server runs ([server] map), as the client-facing level path.

### `node.raw.getServerVersion() -> string`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_server_version`  
Note: e.g. "3.9.3"

The server's version string, e.g. "3.9.3".

### `node.raw.getPort() -> number`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_port`  
Note: TCP+UDP listening port

The port the server listens on (TCP and UDP share it).

### `node.raw.getMaxPlayers() -> number`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_max_players`  
Note: [server] max_players

The player limit currently enforced ([server] max_players, or setMaxPlayers).

### `node.raw.getMaxCars() -> number`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_max_cars`  
Note: [server] cars_per_player

The per-player vehicle limit currently enforced ([server] cars_per_player, or setMaxCars).

### `node.raw.getServerUptime() -> number`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_server_uptime`

Seconds since the server started (monotonic, fractional).

### `node.raw.getUnixTime() -> number`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_unix_time`

Current unix time in whole seconds. node.getTime has the fractional part.

### `node.raw.setMaxCars(n) -> boolean`

Arguments: `n: number`  
C: `set_max_cars`

Changes the per-player vehicle limit live. Enforced on the NEXT spawn request; nobody's existing cars are deleted. Returns false for a negative value.

### `node.raw.setMaxPlayers(n) -> boolean`

Arguments: `n: number`  
C: `set_max_players`

Changes the player limit live. Enforced on the NEXT connect; nobody is kicked. Returns false for a negative value.

### `node.raw.setServerName(name) -> boolean`

Arguments: `name: string`  
C: `set_server_name`

Changes the name the server reports. Returns false for an empty name.

### `node.raw.getMetrics() -> record{connections,memory,net,platform,players,plugin,unixTime,uptime,vehicles}`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_metrics_json`

A snapshot of live server metrics as a table: players, vehicles, uptime, unixTime, net (TCP/UDP packet counters, flood kicks), connections (the connection limiter), plugin (worker queue depth, timer and async task counts), platform and memory (ABI 2.3). Cheap enough to poll from a timer for a status page.

### `node.raw.isNodeGrabEnabled() -> boolean`

Arguments: none  
C: `is_node_grab_enabled`

true when the experimental node grabber is enabled in server.toml ([experimental] node_grab). Even then every grab still needs an explicit allow from the fail-closed vehicleNodeGrabRequest.

### `node.raw.getProtoVersion() -> number`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_proto_version`

The wire protocol version this server speaks (21 at ABI 2.1). The core already kicks a mod whose session:hello names another version; a resource with a check of its own compares against this, never a literal -- a literal drifts the next time the protocol moves.

### `node.raw.getMemory() -> record{lua,luaAll,process}`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_memory_json`

Memory in bytes: lua (this resource's state -- collectgarbage("count") * 1024), luaAll (every loaded resource together), process (the server's resident set; 0 where unknown).

### `node.raw.getPlatform() -> record{arch,os,version}`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_platform_json`

Where the server runs: os ("windows", "linux", "macos"), arch ("x64", "arm64") and version. Constant for the process.

### `node.raw.getHandlerStats(reset?) -> record{byKind,count,max,mean,min,over,stddev,thresholdMs}`

Arguments: `reset?: number?`  
C: `get_handler_stats_json`

This resource's handler timing (milliseconds of own time): count, min, max, mean, stddev, over (runs past thresholdMs = 250), byKind per event/timer/async task. reset = 1 zeroes them after reading.

### `node.raw.shutdownServer(reason?, delayMs) -> boolean`

Arguments: `reason?: string?`, `delayMs: number`  
C: `shutdown_server`

Stops the server like Ctrl+C after delayMs (0 = now), kicking everyone with the reason; a negative delayMs cancels a scheduled stop. Returns false when the server is already stopping or there was nothing to cancel.

### `node.raw.getTime() -> number`

Arguments: none  
*Lua only.*

Unix time in seconds with sub-second precision (a float). node.getUnixTime returns whole seconds.

## Players

Who is connected, what they are called, where they are and what they sit in. Player ids are small integers assigned at connect and REUSED after a disconnect, so never keep one across a playerLeft. Every getter returns nil (Lua) / a failure code (C) for an id that is not connected.

### `node.raw.getPlayerCount() -> number`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_player_count`

How many players are connected right now.

### `node.raw.getPlayerName(playerId) -> string?`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_name`

The player's display name, or nil when no player has that id.

### `node.raw.isPlayerConnected(playerId) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `is_player_connected`

true while a player with this id is connected. Ids are reused after a disconnect, so this cannot tell you whether it is the SAME player you saw earlier -- keep the name or IP for that.

### `node.raw.getPlayerPing(playerId) -> number?`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_ping`

Seconds since the last keepalive arrived from the player (a float), or nil for an unknown id. This is a connection-freshness metric, not a round-trip latency: 0-1 for a healthy client, growing while the client stalls.

### `node.raw.getPlayerIp(playerId) -> string?`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_ip`

The player's remote IP as text ("1.2.3.4"; an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address arrives as plain dotted v4, an IPv6 one as-is), or nil for an unknown id. The basis for banIp and for keying anything you persist about a player.

### `node.raw.getPlayers() -> array<record{id,name}>`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_players_json`

Every connected player as an array of {id, name}. An array rather than a map keyed by id, so the shape is the same in every language.

### `node.raw.getPlayerEndpoint(playerId) -> record{ip,port}?`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_endpoint_json`

The player's remote endpoint as {ip, port}, or nil for an unknown id.

### `node.raw.getPlayerPosition(playerId) -> record{x,y,z}?`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_position_json`

The player's last known position as {x, y, z}, taken from the vehicle it occupies in any role or from its walking avatar, or nil when the player is unknown or no position snapshot has arrived yet.

### `node.raw.getPlayerVehicle(playerId) -> record{gid,role,seat}?`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_vehicle_json`

The single vehicle the player occupies, as {gid, role, seat} with role "driver" or "passenger" and seat the seat id it holds ("" when the car reported no layout), or nil when the player is on foot or unknown.

### `node.raw.getPlayerInputs(playerId) -> record{axes,axesAgeSeconds,controls,controlsAgeSeconds,vehicle}`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_inputs_json`

Everything the player is pressing, in one table: { vehicle = the gid they drive, controls = { steering, throttle, brake, clutch, parkingBrake, gear? } (nil until the first snapshot), controlsAgeSeconds, axes = { name = value, ... } (extra/modded axes; empty when none), axesAgeSeconds }. Controls come from the position snapshot at 60 Hz, axes from the delta-gated Inputs stream. Only a driver's inputs reach the server: nil when the player is on foot or a passenger.

### `node.raw.getPlayerFocus(playerId) -> record{ageSeconds,pos}`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_focus_json`

Where the player is looking from: { pos = { x, y, z }, ageSeconds } -- the head/camera position when the client streams a head pose, otherwise the position of the vehicle it drives. This is the point the server's distance-based relay filter uses for the player. nil for an unknown player or one that has not sent a position yet.

### `node.raw.getPlayerHeadPose(playerId) -> record{ageSeconds,freeCamera,pos,rot}`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_head_pose_json`

The player's last camera/head pose: { pos = { x, y, z }, rot = { x, y, z, w } (a quaternion), freeCamera (true while flying the free camera), ageSeconds }. Streamed at about 10 Hz. nil for an unknown player or one that has not sent a pose yet.

### `node.raw.getPlayerCamera(playerId) -> record{ageSeconds,vehicle}`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_camera_json`

Which vehicle the player's camera is on -- its own, or the one it spectates: { vehicle = gid or -1, ageSeconds } from the client's last camera report, plus denied = true when the session is strict (setPlayerStrict) and the server refused to relay that report because its target is not a vehicle the player occupies (the field is absent otherwise; ABI 1.12). nil for an unknown player or one that has not reported yet.

### `node.raw.getPlayerSession(playerId) -> record{accountRoles,connectedSeconds,focusAgeSeconds,frozen,guest,id,identifiers,ip,lagging,locale,name,overCapacity,pingSeconds,port,role,silentSeconds,synced,udpConnected,unicycle,vehicle,vehicles,verified}`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_session_json`

Everything about the connection in one table: { id, name, ip, port, connectedSeconds, synced (finished joining), udpConnected (the UDP channel is up -- false means positions are falling back to TCP), pingSeconds (since the last keepalive), unicycle (the walking avatar's gid or -1), vehicle (the gid occupied or -1), role ("driver", "passenger" or "none"), vehicles (how many it drives), focusAgeSeconds (-1 when no position was ever seen), verified (true when the join ticket was redeemed with the directory -- the name is then the account's username or the guest name the directory minted, not something the player typed), guest (no account behind the name), accountId (the directory's account id, nil for a guest), accountRoles (the directory's role string such as "ADM", "" for a guest), identifiers (what the directory knows the player by: "nodemp:&lt;id&gt;", "discord:&lt;id&gt;", "ip:&lt;addr&gt;"; empty when unverified), installId (the player's launcher installation as this server knows it, nil without one -- see Player.installId), locale (the game's language, e.g. "ru_RU"; "" until the mod says), overCapacity (it joined a full server), frozen }. nil for an unknown player.

### `node.raw.getPlayerPolicy(playerId) -> record{allowGrab,allowTrigger,lockMode}?`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_policy_json`

The player's vehicle policy as {lockMode, allowTrigger, allowGrab} -- lockMode "unlocked", "locked" or "driver-only" -- or nil until the client has sent one (and for an unknown player).

### `node.raw.freezePlayer(playerId, on) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `on: number`  
C: `freeze_player`

Freezes (on = 1) or thaws (0) the player; on is a number here, the public Player:freeze takes a boolean. Returns false for an unknown player.

## Roles, kicks and bans

Roles are per-session strings a plugin assigns and reads back (they are cleared at disconnect and mean nothing to the server itself; the client shows a role's tag next to the name). Kicks disconnect with a reason. Bans are by IP, by directory account or by launcher installation (the id the directory gives this server for it), persist in bans.json across restarts, and refuse FUTURE connects -- banning an IP does not drop an existing session; banPlayer does both.

### `node.raw.kickPlayer(playerId, reason) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `reason: string`  
C: `kick_player`

Disconnects the player, showing it the reason. Returns false when no player has that id. The disconnect itself completes asynchronously; playerLeft fires when it does.

### `node.raw.setPlayerRole(playerId, role) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `role: string`  
C: `set_player_role`

Assigns a per-session role string, shown by the client as a tag next to the name and readable back with getPlayerRole. Cleared at disconnect. An empty string clears it. Returns false when the player does not exist.

### `node.raw.getPlayerRole(playerId) -> string?`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_role`

The role set by setPlayerRole, nil for an unknown player, "" when none was set.

### `node.raw.banPlayer(playerId, reason?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `reason?: string?`  
C: `ban_player`

Bans the player -- its IP, its account when verified, its launcher installation when the directory named one -- persisted in bans.json, and kicks it with the reason. Returns false when the player does not exist.

### `node.raw.banIp(ip, reason?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `ip: string`, `reason?: string?`  
C: `ban_ip`

Bans FUTURE connects from the IP; an existing session from it stays connected. Returns false for an unparsable IP.

### `node.raw.unbanIp(ip) -> boolean`

Arguments: `ip: string`  
C: `unban_ip`

Lifts a ban. Returns false when the IP was not banned.

### `node.raw.isBanned(ip) -> boolean`

Arguments: `ip: string`  
C: `is_ip_banned`

true while the IP is banned.

### `node.raw.getBans() -> array<record{at,ip,name,reason,until}>`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_bans_json`

Every current ban as an array of {ip, account, installId, reason, at (unix seconds), name (the player's name when banned in-session, "" for a bare banIp), until (when a timed ban ends, nil for good)}. An IP ban has ip set; an account ban has account set (the directory's account id) and ip ""; a launcher installation ban has installId set and ip "".

### `node.raw.banAccount(accountId, reason?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `accountId: number`, `reason?: string?`  
C: `ban_account`

Bans a directory account for future joins, whatever address it comes from; an existing session stays connected (use Player:ban() to do both). Only a server announcing to a directory can tell accounts apart -- without one every player is an unverified guest and this bans nobody. Returns false for a negative id.

### `node.raw.unbanAccount(accountId) -> boolean`

Arguments: `accountId: number`  
C: `unban_account`

Lifts an account ban. Returns false when the account was not banned.

### `node.raw.isAccountBanned(accountId) -> boolean`

Arguments: `accountId: number`  
C: `is_account_banned`

true while the account is banned on this server.

### `node.raw.banPlayerUntil(playerId, reason?, untilUnix) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `reason?: string?`, `untilUnix: number`  
C: `ban_player_until`

Bans the player (IP always, account when verified, launcher installation when named) until untilUnix (unix seconds) and kicks it with the reason. Returns false when the player does not exist.

### `node.raw.banIpUntil(ip, reason?, untilUnix) -> boolean`

Arguments: `ip: string`, `reason?: string?`, `untilUnix: number`  
C: `ban_ip_until`

Bans future connects from the IP until untilUnix (unix seconds). Returns false for an unparsable IP.

### `node.raw.banAccountUntil(accountId, reason?, untilUnix) -> boolean`

Arguments: `accountId: number`, `reason?: string?`, `untilUnix: number`  
C: `ban_account_until`

Bans a directory account for future joins until untilUnix (unix seconds). Returns false for a negative id.

### `node.raw.banInstallId(installId, reason?, untilUnix) -> boolean`

Arguments: `installId: string`, `reason?: string?`, `untilUnix: number`  
C: `ban_install_id`

Bans a launcher installation (player.installId) for future joins until untilUnix (0: for good), whatever account it brings. false when the id is not 64 hex digits.

### `node.raw.unbanInstallId(installId) -> boolean`

Arguments: `installId: string`  
C: `unban_install_id`

Lifts a launcher installation ban. false when it was not banned.

### `node.raw.isInstallIdBanned(installId) -> boolean`

Arguments: `installId: string`  
C: `is_install_id_banned`

true while the launcher installation is banned on this server.

## Strict session: install verification and the camera rule

The server-side half of a strict session (ABI 1.12). Every join already runs the game-install check that [integrity] verify asks for: the player's launcher helper compares the install with the game's own manifest at level size (file lengths), scripts (plus a hash of the game's lua/ and ui/), full (hash everything, archives included -- slow, an audit) or strict (the archive tables of contents against the server's reference manifest, the whole game root, the user folder), and the server judges the report -- a mismatch is a kick unless [integrity] verify is off. That report reaches plugins as the playerVerifyReported notification, after the verdict. player_verify asks the helper to run the check again in the middle of the session, at a level of the plugin's choosing; the answer arrives as another playerVerifyReported. player_set_strict marks a session strict on the server, which changes one relay rule: Vehicle::Camera frames FROM the strict player that target a vehicle it does not drive or occupy are refused -- not relayed -- so a strict client cannot follow other people's cars with its camera; a refused report still fires playerCameraChanged and shows up in get_player_camera_json with "denied": true. Frames from other players to the strict player are unaffected. The check runs on the player's machine, by a launcher the player could patch: it catches damaged installs and casual edits, and it is not a security boundary.

Contract for the server side (what the generated bindings and the prelude rely on): playerVerifyReported is dispatched like playerSeatChanged -- the three-argument notification form with global_id -1 and the report as JSON -- for the join-time report and for every mid-session one, after the server's own verdict; player_verify counts a request as pending for 60 s from the moment it was sent, report or no report, and answers -3 inside that window -- the check every join runs does not count, so a player_verify from a playerJoined handler goes out at once; get_player_camera_json writes "denied": true only for a refused frame of the player's own and leaves the key out otherwise, so the record's shape is unchanged for everyone else.

### `node.raw.verifyPlayer(playerId, level) -> number`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `level: number`  
C: `player_verify`

Asks the player's launcher helper to re-check the game install at level 1 (size), 2 (scripts), 3 (full) or 4 (strict); the result arrives as the playerVerifyReported event, after the server's own verdict on it. Returns 0 when the request went out, -1 unknown player, -2 the level is not available for this player (launcher too old, or strict without a reference manifest on this server), -3 a request to this player is still pending (one per player per 60 s; the check every join runs does not count, so a call from a playerJoined handler goes out at once). The public Player:verify takes the level as a string and turns these into true / nil, reason.

### `node.raw.setPlayerStrict(playerId, on) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `on: number`  
C: `player_set_strict`

Marks the player's session strict (on = 1) or lifts the mark (0): a Vehicle::Camera frame from the player that targets a vehicle it does not occupy is then refused (not relayed; frames from others to it are unaffected), and the refused report shows up in getPlayerCamera with denied = true. Returns false when the player does not exist. on is a number here, as in C; the public Player:setStrict takes a boolean.

## Vehicles: registry and records

The vehicle registry: global ids, who spawned and who drives each one, the occupants, and the full JSON record. Global ids are unique for the life of the server process. A vehicle's spawner may be a player or -1 for a server-spawned one; its sync AUTHORITY is the client that simulates it and is not necessarily the spawner.

### `node.raw.getVehicleCount() -> number`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_vehicle_count`

How many vehicles are in the registry, unicycles (players on foot) included.

### `node.raw.getPlayerVehicles(playerId) -> array<number>`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_vehicles`

Global ids of the vehicles the player spawned or is driving, as an array (empty for an unknown player). Up to 512.

### `node.raw.getVehicle(globalId) -> record{authority,configHash,damageBlobCounter,damageBlobSize,damageCounter,driver,id,idleSeconds,isUnicycle,lockMode,lockWhitelist,name,partsHash,passengers,seats,spawner,spawnerOnline,tags}?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_json`

The complete vehicle record: id, spawner (player id, -1 for server-spawned), driver (-1 when empty), authority (the client simulating it), isUnicycle, name, idleSeconds, passengers (array of player ids), seats (wire v24: the seat layout the owner reported, in its order, as {id, driver, playerId} with playerId -1 for a free seat; empty when it reported none), spawnerOnline, lockMode, lockWhitelist, configHash, damageCounter, damageBlobCounter, damageBlobSize, tags (key -> value). nil for an unknown vehicle.

### `node.raw.getVehicles() -> array<record{authority,configHash,damageBlobCounter,damageBlobSize,damageCounter,driver,id,idleSeconds,isUnicycle,lockMode,lockWhitelist,name,partsHash,passengers,seats,spawner,spawnerOnline,tags}>`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_vehicles_json`

Every vehicle's record (the shape node.getVehicle returns) as an array. Unicycles included -- filter on isUnicycle for cars only.

### `node.raw.getVehicleOccupants(globalId) -> array<record{playerId,role,seat}>`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_occupants_json`

Who is in the vehicle, driver first, as an array of {playerId, role, seat}. Empty for an empty or unknown vehicle.

## Vehicles: state, live and cached

What the server knows about a vehicle's state. LIVE state (transform, controls) comes from the last position/state snapshot the authority sent and is a few ticks old. CACHED state (config, couplers, powertrain, engine, controllers, electrics, damage) is the merge of everything relayed so far and is what a late joiner receives. Config hash is the FNV-1a-32 marker clients echo so the server can spot a desynced config.

### `node.raw.getVehicleElectrics(globalId) -> record{electrics}?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_electrics_json`

The vehicle's current electrics -- lights, signals, gauges, the merged view of every electrics delta relayed so far -- as a table, or nil when the vehicle does not exist or nothing has arrived yet.

### `node.raw.getVehicleConfig(globalId) -> record{jbm,vcf}?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_config_json`

The vehicle's current spawn/edit configuration as a table -- exactly the JSON a joiner receives inside Vehicle::Spawn (jbm, vcf, ...) -- or nil for an unknown vehicle.

### `node.raw.getVehicleCouplers(globalId) -> array<record{name,state}>?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_couplers_json`

The merged coupler/door state as an array of coupler entries in the client Coupler schema (one per known coupler), or nil for an unknown vehicle.

### `node.raw.getVehiclePowertrain(globalId) -> record{powertrain}?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_powertrain_json`

The merged powertrain device state as a table, or nil while nothing has arrived (or the merged store was invalidated by an unknown schema).

### `node.raw.getVehicleEngine(globalId) -> record{engine}?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_engine_json`

The merged engine device state as a table, or nil while nothing has arrived.

### `node.raw.getVehicleControllers(globalId) -> record{probe|setState}?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_controllers_json`

The merged controller-call map as a table of "controller|function" -> the last call (the same shape node.triggerVehicle takes), or nil for an unknown vehicle.

### `node.raw.getWorldSnapshot() -> record{players,time,vehicles}`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_world_snapshot_json`

One bulk read of the world: {time = unix seconds, players = [{id, name, ip, vehicle, role, pos = {x, y, z} or nil}], vehicles = [{id, spawner, driver, authority, unicycle, pos, rot, vel}]}. The read for radars, maps and dashboards -- one call instead of a loop of getters.

### `node.raw.getVehicleConfigHash(globalId) -> number?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_config_hash`

The vehicle's config marker: FNV-1a-32 over the cached config JSON bytes exactly as Spawn and Resync send them -- what clients echo in their periodic ConfigHash reports, so a mismatch means that client's config diverged. nil for an unknown vehicle.

### `node.raw.getVehicleDamage(globalId) -> record{blob,blobCounter,counter,size}?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_damage_json`

Damage telemetry: {counter (the authority-reported damage version), blobCounter (the version the stored blob was made at), size (0 = pristine), blob (the raw damage bytes as a binary string, or nil when pristine)}. nil for an unknown vehicle.

### `node.raw.getVehicleTransform(globalId) -> record{ageSeconds,angVel,hasControls,paused,ping,pos,rot,seq,teleport,time,transitioning,vel,velocityStep}?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_transform_json`

Kinematics from the last position snapshot: {pos = {x, y, z}, rot (a quaternion {x, y, z, w}), vel, angVel, time, ping, seq, paused, transitioning, teleport, velocityStep, hasControls, ageSeconds}. Velocities in m/s, angular velocity in rad/s, time and ping the sender's own timers in seconds, ageSeconds since the server accepted the snapshot. teleport and velocityStep are the sender's step flags for this snapshot -- a reset, a recovery, a server teleport -- so a plugin tracking movement can tell a jump from driving; hasControls says whether getVehicleControls has meaningful values for it. nil while no snapshot arrived. A few ticks old by construction.

### `node.raw.getVehicleControls(globalId) -> record{brake,clutch,parkingBrake,steering,throttle}?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_controls_json`

Driving inputs from the same snapshot: {steering, throttle, brake, clutch, parkingBrake, gear?} -- gear a number for manual boxes, a letter for automatics ("P", "R", "N", "D", "S", "2", "1", "M3") when the sender reported one. nil while no snapshot arrived.

### `node.raw.getVehicleInputs(globalId) -> record{ageSeconds,axes}`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_inputs_json`

The driver's extra input axes: { axes = { name = value, ... }, ageSeconds }. Only axes that are NOT in the position snapshot travel here (modded or extra inputs; steering, throttle, brake, clutch, parking brake and gear are getVehicleControls). The stream is delta-gated -- an axis is sent only when it changes -- and merged on the server, so this is the current set, not the last delta; ageSeconds is since the last change arrived. nil when the vehicle is unknown or no axes were ever sent (a stock car sends none).

### `node.raw.getVehicleNodes(globalId) -> record{nodes}`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_nodes_json`

The last node-position packet the vehicle's authority sent, decoded as it was sent (the client's nodemp.sync.nodes schema -- deformation / node positions). Intermittent: it arrives when the client decides the deformation changed enough. nil when none arrived yet.

### `node.raw.getVehicleBreakGroups(globalId) -> array<string>`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_break_groups_json`

The names of every break group reported broken on the vehicle (bumpers, glass, parts that detach), merged from the delta stream into the current set. Empty array for an intact car that has reported; nil when the vehicle is unknown or nothing arrived yet.

### `node.raw.getVehiclePaints(globalId) -> array<record{baseColor}>`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_paints_json`

The vehicle's paints: an array of paint layers in the client vcf.paints schema (baseColor and the material fields), taken from the cached config, into which every Paint packet is merged. Empty for a config without paints; nil for an unknown vehicle. The same data sits at getVehicleConfig(gid).vcf.paints -- this is the direct read.

### `node.raw.getVehicleSync(globalId) -> record{authority,authorityAgeSeconds,configChangedAgeSeconds,hasPos,idleSeconds,posAgeSeconds,posSeq,seatEpoch,spawnedAgeSeconds}`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_sync_json`

How alive the vehicle's sync is: { authority (the player simulating it, -1 none), authorityAgeSeconds (since its last accepted position packet or its assignment; the heartbeat the server's grace/release logic reads), seatEpoch (bumped on every seat or authority change), spawnedAgeSeconds, idleSeconds (0 while someone drives), configChangedAgeSeconds (since the last edit/paint/spawn), hasPos, posAgeSeconds, posSeq }. The record for a 'why is this car frozen' tool. nil for an unknown vehicle.

### `node.raw.getVehicleTransforms() -> array<record{ageSeconds,angVel,controls,electrics,hasControls,id,paused,ping,pos,rot,seq,teleport,time,transitioning,vel,velocityStep}>`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_vehicle_transforms_json`

The positions table: every vehicle's last kinematic snapshot in one array, each entry the shape of getVehicleTransform plus id, ascending by id. Each entry also carries controls (only when hasControls) and electrics (only when the car has sent one), so a telemetry recorder or dashboard reads the FULL state in one call instead of vehicle:controls() and vehicle:electrics() per car. One lock, no per-vehicle copies -- the read for a radar, a minimap or a replay recorder polling every tick. Vehicles that have not sent a position yet are absent; an empty array when none has.

### `node.raw.getVehicleTransformsSelect(select?) -> array<record{ageSeconds,angVel,controls,electrics,hasControls,id,paused,ping,pos,rot,seq,teleport,time,transitioning,vel,velocityStep}>`

Arguments: `select?: table?`  
C: `get_vehicle_transforms_select_json`

The positions table with only what you read: getVehicleTransforms when select is nil, otherwise {"pos", "vel", ...} or {fields = {...}} picks the record keys, electrics = {"rpm", ...} copies only those electrics keys (true/false: all/none), controls = true/false, ids = {...} reads only those vehicles. id is always there; unknown keys are ignored. A tick that needs positions alone pays for positions alone.

## Vehicles: control

Acting on vehicles from the server: delete, spawn ownerless vehicles, open and close couplers, seat, unseat and swap players, lock vehicles against new seat claims, execute a controller call on the sync authority, and resync a vehicle or a player. Server-driven seating bypasses locks; locks gate NEW client claims only and never evict anyone.

### `node.raw.deleteVehicle(globalId) -> boolean`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `delete_vehicle`

Deletes the vehicle: every client removes it, it leaves the registry, and vehicleDeleted fires (asynchronously, on the worker). Returns false when there is no such vehicle.

### `node.raw.deleteAllVehicles() -> number`

Arguments: none  
C: `delete_all_vehicles`

Deletes every vehicle in the world and returns how many that was. Players end up on foot.

### `node.raw.setVehicleCoupler(globalId, name, open) -> boolean`

Arguments: `globalId: number`, `name: string`, `open: boolean`  
C: `set_vehicle_coupler`

Opens (true) or closes (false) the named advanced-coupler group -- "doorFLCoupler", "hood_latch_coupler" and the like -- on EVERY client, the driver's included, and merges the new state into the join-replay cache so late joiners see it. Returns false when the vehicle does not exist. This is the server-driven counterpart of a player pulling a door handle (vehicleCouplerRequest).

### `node.raw.unseatPlayer(playerId) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `unseat_player`

Forces the player out of the vehicle it occupies as driver or passenger; it ends up on foot, the client spawning its unicycle as on a normal exit. Returns false when the player is already on foot or unknown.

### `node.raw.swapPlayers(playerA, playerB) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerA: number`, `playerB: number`  
C: `swap_players`

Exchanges the two players' seats -- role AND vehicle -- in one transaction, covering the whole driver/passenger/on-foot matrix: two drivers swap cars, a driver and a passenger of one car exchange roles, a seat and a pedestrian trade places. Returns false when either player is unknown or both are on foot.

### `node.raw.resyncVehicle(globalId, playerId?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `globalId: number`, `playerId?: number?`  
C: `resync_vehicle`

Re-sends the vehicle's spawn and cached state to one player, or to everyone when playerId is omitted -- the cure for a client whose copy diverged. Returns false for an unknown vehicle.

### `node.raw.resyncPlayer(playerId) -> number`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `resync_player`

Re-sends EVERY vehicle's spawn and cached state to one player -- the cure for a client whose world diverged -- and returns how many vehicle bundles were sent.

### `node.raw.spawnVehicle(configJson, ownerName?) -> number?`

Arguments: `configJson: string`, `ownerName?: string?`  
C: `spawn_vehicle`

Spawns an OWNERLESS vehicle (spawner -1, empty driver seat) and hands sync authority to the best available client. configJson is a JSON object in the client SpawnReq schema (jbm, vcf, pos, rot, ...); ownerName is a display label. Returns the new global id, or nil when nobody can host it or the config is rejected.

### `node.raw.triggerVehicle(globalId, call) -> boolean`

Arguments: `globalId: number`, `call: any`  
C: `trigger_vehicle`

Has the vehicle's SYNC AUTHORITY execute one controller call -- a table or a JSON object string in the Vehicle::Trigger schema {controllerName, functionName, variables...}. Skips vehicleTriggerRequest (the server never asks itself) and is never cached: the resulting state rides the normal sync. Returns false for an unknown vehicle or one without an authority.

### `node.raw.getVehicleLock(globalId) -> record{accounts,mode,owner,whitelist}?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_lock_json`

The vehicle's lock as {mode, whitelist, owner}: mode "unlocked", "locked" or "driver-only"; whitelist the player ids always allowed in; owner "plugin" (set by a resource; the spawner's policy leaves it alone) or "policy". nil for an unknown vehicle.

### `node.raw.setVehicleLock(globalId, mode, whitelist?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `globalId: number`, `mode: string`, `whitelist?: table?`  
C: `set_vehicle_lock_named`

Locks the vehicle against NEW client seat claims. mode is "unlocked", "locked" or "driver-only" (case-insensitive; "driveronly"/"driver_only" accepted); whitelist is a JSON array of up to 128 entries (pids and accounts together; ABI 2.2, was 32) that are always allowed, ignored for "unlocked": an integer is a player id for THIS session; {"account": <directory account id, number or digit string>} names whoever is connected with that account, so a holder who reconnects with a new pid needs no new call. The Vehicle::Lock hint sent to clients carries the pids plus the pids of ONLINE account matches, re-sent when such a holder joins; the claim check itself matches the account directly. Nobody already seated is evicted, and server-driven seating bypasses locks. Returns false for an unknown vehicle, an unknown mode or an over-limit whitelist.

### `node.raw.seatPlayer(playerId, globalId, role?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `globalId: number`, `role?: string?`  
C: `seat_player_role`

Seats the player in the vehicle as "driver" (default) or "passenger", bypassing locks; the previous driver, if any, becomes a passenger. Returns false when either does not exist or the player is not joined.

### `node.raw.releaseVehicleLock(globalId) -> boolean`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `release_vehicle_lock`

Hands a plugin-locked vehicle back to its spawner's own setting: the spawner's lock mode with the spawner whitelisted (unlocked when the spawner is gone). false for an unknown vehicle.

### `node.raw.resetVehicle(globalId, transform?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `globalId: number`, `transform?: any?`  
C: `reset_vehicle`

Repairs the vehicle in place (no argument: at its last known position) or resets it to transform = {pos = {x,y,z}, rot = {x,y,z,w}?}. Every client applies it, the driver included; vehicleReset fires with cause "server". false for an unknown vehicle or no known position yet.

### `node.raw.spawnVehicleForPlayer(playerId, config, transform?) -> number`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `config: any`, `transform?: any?`  
C: `spawn_vehicle_for_player`

Spawns a car for the player from a server config and seats them in it. Returns the global id, or a negative code: -1 not joined, -2 cars_per_player, -3 bad config. player:spawnVehicle wraps it.

### `node.raw.teleportVehicle(globalId, transform) -> boolean`

Arguments: `globalId: number`, `transform: any`  
C: `teleport_vehicle`

Moves the vehicle to transform = {pos = {x,y,z}, rot = {x,y,z,w}?} without repairing it (no rot: it keeps facing the same way). false for an unknown vehicle or a bad transform.

### `node.raw.setVehicleQueue(playerId, mode) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `mode: string`  
C: `set_vehicle_queue`

Sets the scheduled-changes mode for everyone (playerId -1: "allow" or "off") or one player ("allow", "off", "" = the server's). false for an unknown player or mode.

### `node.raw.setVehicleQueueMaxDelay(seconds) -> boolean`

Arguments: `seconds: number`  
C: `set_vehicle_queue_max_delay`

Every client builds what waits for it within seconds of the first change arriving (0: no limit). false outside 0..3600.

### `node.raw.applyScheduledVehicles(playerId) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `apply_scheduled_vehicles`

Every client (playerId -1) or one builds what waits for it now; the edits the server still holds go first. false for an unknown player.

## Vehicle tags

Key/value strings a plugin attaches to any vehicle. Tags survive edits, resets and respawns, ride the join replay to late joiners and are broadcast to every client on change (read-only client-side, readable via the vehicle record). Limits: key 1-64 bytes, value 1-256 bytes, 32 distinct keys per vehicle -- a set that breaks a limit is rejected, never truncated.

### `node.raw.setVehicleTag(globalId, key, value) -> boolean`

Arguments: `globalId: number`, `key: string`, `value: string`  
C: `set_vehicle_tag`

Sets or overwrites one tag on the vehicle. Returns false when the vehicle does not exist or a limit is broken: empty key or value, key over 64 bytes, value over 256 bytes, or a 33rd distinct key -- rejected, never truncated. An empty value is not a legal tag; use removeVehicleTag. Fires vehicleTagsChanged and broadcasts the tag set to every client.

### `node.raw.getVehicleTag(globalId, key) -> string?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`, `key: string`  
C: `get_vehicle_tag`

The value of one tag, or nil when the vehicle or the tag does not exist.

### `node.raw.removeVehicleTag(globalId, key) -> boolean`

Arguments: `globalId: number`, `key: string`  
C: `remove_vehicle_tag`

Removes one tag. Returns true when it existed and was removed, false otherwise. Fires vehicleTagsChanged when it did.

### `node.raw.getVehicleTags(globalId) -> record{probe}?`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_tags_json`

The vehicle's whole tag set as a table of key -> value (the measured shape shows a sample key; it is a plain map), or nil when the vehicle does not exist. Also available as the `tags` field of node.getVehicle.

### `node.raw.getVehiclesByTag(key, value?) -> array<number>`

Arguments: `key: string`, `value?: string?`  
C: `get_vehicles_by_tag`

Global ids (ascending) of every vehicle carrying the tag key -- with any value, or exactly value when given. Empty array when none. For a several-pairs AND query use getVehiclesByTags.

### `node.raw.getVehiclesByTags(pairs) -> array<number>`

Arguments: `pairs: table`  
C: `get_vehicles_by_tags`

Global ids (ascending) of every vehicle whose tag set matches EVERY pair in the given table {key = value, ...} -- an AND query. Values may be strings or numbers (numbers compare as their string form); an empty table matches nothing.

## Visibility: relay filter and groups

Deciding who sees what. The relayRequest filter (formerly canRelay) answers, per (sender, receiver, packet category, subtype, vehicle), whether a packet is forwarded; verdicts are cached until invalidated. Visibility groups are the coarse form: a player only receives relays from players and vehicles in the same group (group 0 is everyone). The dimensions module is built on groups.

### `node.raw.invalidateRelayCache() -> nil`

Arguments: none  
C: `invalidate_relay_cache`

Drops every cached relayRequest verdict so the hooks are consulted again. Call it whenever the data your relayRequest hook reads has changed; until you do, the old answers stand. Thread-safe.

### `node.raw.setPlayerGroup(playerId, group) -> nil`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `group: number`  
C: `set_player_group`

Puts the player in a visibility group. Two things see each other only when their group numbers match, and 0 -- the default -- is the world everyone shares. That one rule covers parallel worlds, instanced races, private lobbies and spectator rooms. Takes effect on the next relayed packet; a player moving groups sees the other group's vehicles disappear and its own appear.

### `node.raw.setVehicleGroup(globalId, group) -> nil`

Arguments: `globalId: number`, `group: number`  
C: `set_vehicle_group`

Puts the vehicle in a visibility group (0 = shared world). A packet about a vehicle belongs to the VEHICLE's group, not its driver's, because a car can be moved between worlds with someone in it. Forgotten when the vehicle is deleted.

### `node.raw.getPlayerGroup(playerId) -> number`

Arguments: `playerId: number`  
C: `get_player_group`

The player's visibility group; 0 when never set or the player is unknown.

### `node.raw.getVehicleGroup(globalId) -> number`

Arguments: `globalId: number`  
C: `get_vehicle_group`

The vehicle's visibility group; 0 when never set or there is no such vehicle.

## Events: subscribe and emit

The event system has three kinds of name. ENGINE events are camelCase builtins the server fires, named `<subject><Verb-ed>` (playerJoined, vehicleSpawned); the requests a handler can deny are named `<subject><Action>Request` (playerConnectRequest, vehicleSpawnRequest; relayRequest is the relay filter), and a handler that returns false denies the action. WIRE events are `<domain>:<verb>` strings a resource defines itself, sent with emitClient/emitAll and received from clients through the same node.on. One node.on covers all of them: the name decides the kind. The pre-1.2.0 names (playerJoin, onPlayerConnectRequest, canRelay, ...) are GONE: subscribing to one is refused with an error naming the new spelling, rather than silently becoming a wire subscription that never fires. Each event's *Formerly* line records the name it was renamed from.

### `node.raw.emitClient(playerId, event, data) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `event: string`, `data: string`  
C: `emit_client`

Sends a wire event to one player: event is a `<domain>:<verb>` name, data a string (by convention JSON -- node.jsonEncode). Returns false when the player is not connected. The client receives it in any handler registered with node.on(event, fn) on its side.

### `node.raw.emitAll(event, data) -> nil`

Arguments: `event: string`, `data: string`  
C: `emit_all`

Sends a wire event to every connected player, subject to the relayRequest filter and visibility groups like any relay.

### `node.raw.on(name, fn) -> nil`

Arguments: `name: string`, `fn: function`  
C: `register_client_event`, `register_builtin_event`, `register_cancellable_event`, `register_vehicle_event`, `register_relay_filter`

Subscribes fn to an event by name. Builtin engine events (playerJoined, vehicleSpawned...), cancellable requests (...Request), vehicle notifications and the relayRequest hook are recognised by name; ANY other name is a wire event a client may send with node.emitServer. Handler arguments depend on the kind -- see the events reference. Several handlers per name are fine, across resources too; each resource's handlers are dropped when it unloads. This raw form appends: subscribing the same function twice registers it twice (it runs twice; off(name, fn) removes both). The public node.on replaces the first subscription instead. A name this event was renamed FROM (playerJoin, onVehicleSpawnRequest, canRelay, ...) is refused here with an error naming the new spelling: it is not an alias and does not subscribe to anything.

### `node.raw.off(name, fn?) -> number`

Arguments: `name: string`, `fn?: function?`  
C: `unregister_client_event`, `unregister_builtin_event`, `unregister_cancellable_event`, `unregister_vehicle_event`, `unregister_relay_filter`

Unsubscribes this resource's handlers for the name -- all of them, or only fn when given -- and returns how many were removed. fn is matched by identity against what on() received, from any context, including coroutines (node.async, pg transaction bodies). A dispatch already in flight on the worker may still deliver one final call. A name an event was renamed FROM is refused like in on() (the same once-per-resource line) and removes nothing -- nothing could have subscribed to it.

### `node.raw.emitOthers(exceptPlayerId, event, data) -> boolean`

Arguments: `exceptPlayerId: number`, `event: string`, `data: string`  
C: `emit_others`

Sends a wire event to every connected player except one -- the player whose action you are relaying, typically. The fan-out is emitAll's, with exceptPlayerId as the sender for the relay filter (relayRequest sees from = exceptPlayerId) and visibility groups. Returns false, and sends nothing, when exceptPlayerId is not a connected player. Before this the pattern was a loop over getPlayers with emitClient, which skipped the group filter emitAll applies.

## Module channel (binary)

A raw binary channel keyed by a u32 id, for native modules and their client counterparts that speak their own encoding. Nothing is parsed or logged; use it when JSON events are too slow or too loose. The dimensions module owns channel 0x44494D53 ("DIMS").

### `node.raw.sendModule(playerId, channel, data) -> boolean`

Arguments: `playerId: number`, `channel: number`, `data: string`  
C: `send_module`

One binary payload to one player on a u32 channel, or to everyone (relayRequest-respecting) when playerId is -1. data is an arbitrary binary string. Returns false when the player is not connected.

### `node.raw.onModule(channel, fn) -> nil`

Arguments: `channel: number`, `fn: function`  
C: `register_module_channel`

Subscribes fn(playerId, data) to inbound binary payloads clients send on the u32 channel. data is a binary string; nothing is parsed or logged.

### `node.raw.offModule(channel, fn?) -> number`

Arguments: `channel: number`, `fn?: function?`  
C: `unregister_module_channel`

Unsubscribes this resource's handlers for the channel -- all, or only fn -- and returns how many were removed. A dispatch already in flight may deliver one final call.

## Inter-resource bus

Server-side publish/subscribe between resources (and native modules). Delivery is asynchronous on the plugin worker and includes the sender. This is how a resource asks the dimensions module which players share a room. One name belongs to the core's chat (server 1.6.0): chat:say `{ text, pid? }` is rendered as a system line (node.chat.say / tell publish it).

### `node.raw.emitResource(name, data?) -> nil`

Arguments: `name: string`, `data?: string?`  
C: `emit_resource_event`

Publishes a message on the inter-resource bus: every resource and native module subscribed to name receives (sourceResourceName, data) asynchronously on the worker -- the sender included. data is a string (by convention JSON), "" when omitted.

### `node.raw.onResource(name, fn) -> nil`

Arguments: `name: string`, `fn: function`  
C: `register_resource_event`

Subscribes fn(sourceResourceName, data) to bus messages published under name by any resource or native module (a native module appears as source "native").

### `node.raw.offResource(name, fn?) -> number`

Arguments: `name: string`, `fn?: function?`  
C: `unregister_resource_event`

Unsubscribes this resource's bus handlers for name -- all, or only fn -- and returns how many were removed.

### `node.raw.callResource(name, data?) -> (array<record{source,value}>, nil)|(table:empty, nil)`

Arguments: `name: string`, `data?: string?`  
C: `call_resource_event`

Asks every subscriber of name now and returns their answers, in order, as an array of { source, value }. A nil answer is left out. Raises when the calls nest deeper than 8.

## Timers and scheduling

One-shot and repeating timers on the plugin worker thread -- the same thread every handler runs on, so a timer callback never races a handler. setImmediate runs after the current batch of handlers.

### `node.raw.setTimeout(ms, fn) -> number`

Arguments: `ms: number`, `fn: function`  
C: `set_timeout`

Runs fn once after ms milliseconds on the worker thread and returns a positive timer id for clearTimer. A pending timer never fires after the resource unloads or the server stops.

### `node.raw.setInterval(ms, fn, opts?) -> number`

Arguments: `ms: number`, `fn: function`, `opts?: table?`  
C: `set_interval`

Runs fn every ms milliseconds on the worker thread until clearTimer, returning a positive timer id. A slow callback delays the next run; intervals do not stack. opts { precise = true, maxBacklog = n } keeps the schedule instead (server 1.11.0): the runs a busy worker missed follow back to back, each called with the number missed.

### `node.raw.clearTimer(timerId) -> nil`

Arguments: `timerId: number`  
C: `clear_timer`

Cancels a timer from setTimeout or setInterval. Safe for an id that already fired or never existed. A callback mid-execution finishes; an interval will not fire again.

### `node.raw.setImmediate(fn) -> nil`

Arguments: `fn: function`  
*Lua only.*

Runs fn on the worker as soon as the current batch of handlers has finished. Use it to act after every handler of the event you are in has seen it.

## Coroutines and background jobs

Two ways to not block the worker. node.async runs a function as a cooperative coroutine: node.sleep and node.wait suspend it while other handlers and the tick keep running (the FiveM Citizen.Wait model). node.job / node.await run a SELF-CONTAINED function on a background pool thread in a scratch Lua state, for real parallelism: no upvalues, no resource globals, JSON-serialisable arguments and result.

### `node.raw.job(workFn, args?, doneFn?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `workFn: function`, `args?: any`, `doneFn: function?`  
C: `submit_job`

Runs workFn(args) on a BACKGROUND pool thread in a fresh scratch Lua state -- the resource state is never touched off-thread -- and then doneFn(result, errMsg?) on the worker. workFn must be self-contained: no upvalues, no resource globals (it is dumped and reloaded into the scratch state); args and the result must be JSON-serialisable. Returns false if workFn could not be dumped. For linear code use node.await inside node.async.

### `node.raw.async(fn, ...) -> number`

Arguments: `fn: function`, `...: ...`  
*Lua only.*

Runs fn(...) as a cooperative coroutine on the worker thread and returns its task id. Inside it node.sleep, node.wait, node.await and node.yield suspend the coroutine WITHOUT blocking other handlers or the server tick. The task ends when fn returns or errors (the error is logged under the resource's tag).

### `node.raw.sleep(ms) -> nil`

Arguments: `ms: number`  
*Lua only.*

Suspends the CURRENT node.async coroutine for ms milliseconds. Only callable inside one: elsewhere Lua raises its standard "attempt to yield" error.

### `node.raw.wait(msOrPredicate, intervalMs?) -> nil`

Arguments: `msOrPredicate: number|function`, `intervalMs?: number?`  
*Lua only.*

A number sleeps like node.sleep. A function suspends the coroutine until it returns truthy, polled on the worker every intervalMs (default 50). Only callable inside node.async.

### `node.raw.suspend(arm) -> nil`

Arguments: `arm: function`  
*Lua only.*

Suspends the current `node.async` coroutine and hands somebody else the decision about when it continues. `arm(resume)` is called at once, before the coroutine stops; whatever calls `resume(...)` later -- an event handler, a timer, a module answering on the bus -- continues the coroutine, and the values given to `resume` become the return values of `node.suspend`.

This is the general form of `node.sleep` (a timer resumes it) and `node.await` (a pool job resumes it), and it is what a library built on callbacks needs to offer linear code: arm the callback, suspend, return what the callback brought. The database module's `db.lua` is written on it.

`resume` is single-shot and belongs to this one suspension: calling it twice, or after the coroutine has ended, answers false and does nothing. Call it on the worker thread -- where every handler, timer and completion already runs. If `arm` itself raises, the coroutine is resumed with no values rather than left suspended forever.

```lua
node.async(function()
    local answer = node.suspend(function(resume)
        node.bus.emit("db:request", { op = "query", reply = "db:reply:mine", id = 7, sql = "select 1" })
        node.bus.on("db:reply:mine", function(_, data) resume(node.json.decode(data)) end)
    end)
    node.log("the database said %s", node.json.encode(answer))
end)
```

### `node.raw.yield() -> nil`

Arguments: none  
*Lua only.*

Gives the worker back to other handlers and ticks for one turn, then resumes. For long loops inside node.async that MUST run on the worker because they touch live state -- this is fairness, not parallelism; for CPU offload use node.await.

### `node.raw.await(workFn, args?) -> any,string?`

Arguments: `workFn: function`, `args?: any?`  
*Lua only.*

The coroutine form of node.job: runs workFn(args) on a background pool thread in a fresh scratch Lua state and suspends the coroutine until it finishes, then returns the JSON-serialisable result, or nil plus an error message. Same rules as node.job: workFn must be self-contained (no upvalues, no resource globals). Only callable inside node.async. Other handlers and the tick keep running meanwhile: linear code, real parallelism.

## HTTP

Asynchronous HTTP requests from the background pool; the callback runs on the worker. Any method (GET, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE, HEAD, or a custom uppercase token). About 15 s timeout, 8 MB body cap, 5 redirects. TLS peer verification is on: the certificate must chain to the machine's roots plus the shipped cacert.pem -- or to the bundle the hoster names in `[http] ca_file` -- and name the host; a failure comes back as status -1 with the TLS error in body. `[http] insecure` turns verification off.

### `node.raw.httpGet(url, headers?, callback?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `url: string`, `headers?: any`, `callback: function?`  
C: `http_request`

GET url with optional headers (a table of header -> value); callback(status, body, headers) runs on the worker when the request completes: status is the HTTP status code, or -1 with the error text in body when the request failed (could not resolve or connect, TLS handshake or certificate verification, timeout, malformed response, body over the cap). Returns false if the request could not be queued (the background pool is full or shutting down); the callback then never runs. About 15 s timeout, 8 MB body cap, 5 redirects; the peer certificate is verified unless [http] insecure is set. node.httpPost is the POST form, node.httpRequest takes any method.

### `node.raw.httpPost(url, body, headers?, callback?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `url: string`, `body: string`, `headers?: table?`, `callback: function?`  
*Lua only.*

POST body to url with optional headers (a table of header -> value); the callback receives (status, body, headers) on the worker: the HTTP status code, or -1 with the error text in body when the request failed, as for httpGet. Returns false if the request could not be queued (the callback then never runs). Same limits as httpGet: about 15 s timeout, 8 MB body cap, 5 redirects; the peer certificate is verified unless [http] insecure is set.

### `node.raw.httpRequest(method, url, headers?, body?, callback?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `method: string`, `url: string`, `headers?: table?`, `body?: string?`, `callback: function?`  
*Lua only.*

The general form (server 1.2.0): method is a token of uppercase letters A-Z only, at most 16 -- "GET", "POST", "PUT", "PATCH", "DELETE", "HEAD" or a custom one; anything else comes back as status -1 "invalid HTTP method" (the public node.http.request upper-cases for you, this raw form does not) -- url, headers (a table of header -> value, or nil), body (a string, or nil for none) and the callback(status, body, headers) on the worker: the HTTP status code, or -1 with the error text in body when the request failed, exactly as for httpGet/httpPost. A HEAD response has status and headers and an empty body. Returns false if the request could not be queued (the callback then never runs). Same limits: about 15 s timeout, 8 MB body cap, 5 redirects (a 301/302 answer to anything but GET/HEAD is re-issued as GET, a 303 always is); the peer certificate is verified unless [http] insecure is set. httpGet and httpPost are this with the method fixed; the public node.http.request/put/patch/delete/head are built on it.

## Files, sandboxed

Reading, writing and listing files inside the resource's own folder. Absolute paths and any `..` step are rejected. Writes are synchronous unless writeFileAsync is used, which goes through the file-writer thread and coalesces with storage writes.

### `node.raw.readFile(path) -> string?`

Arguments: `path: string`  
*Lua only.*

Reads a file inside the resource's folder; nil if it does not exist or the path escapes the sandbox (absolute paths and any `..` step are rejected).

### `node.raw.writeFile(path, data) -> boolean`

Arguments: `path: string`, `data: string`  
*Lua only.*

Writes data to a file inside the resource's folder, creating parent folders. Synchronous; false if the path is rejected or the write fails.

### `node.raw.writeFileAsync(path, data, cb?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `path: string`, `data: string`, `cb?: function?`  
*Lua only.*

Like writeFile but the disk write happens off the worker on the file-writer thread, coalesced per path with storage writes; the optional cb(ok) runs back on the worker when it completes. Returns false only when the sandbox rejects the path -- true means accepted, not written.

### `node.raw.listFiles(path?) -> array<record{name,dir,size}>?`

Arguments: `path?: string?`  
*Lua only.*

Lists a folder inside the resource's folder (the resource root when omitted): an array of {name, dir (boolean), size}; nil if the path is rejected or does not exist.

### `node.raw.fileExists(path) -> string?`

Arguments: `path: string`  
*Lua only.*

"file", "dir" or nil for a path inside the resource's folder -- without reading it (a stat). nil, "outside" for an absolute path or a `..` step, so a missing file and an escaped path are told apart (ABI 2.3).

### `node.raw.fileStat(path) -> record{dir,mtime,size}?`

Arguments: `path: string`  
*Lua only.*

{ dir = boolean, size = bytes (0 for a folder), mtime = unix seconds with a fraction } for a path inside the resource's folder, or nil, err ("outside" / "not found") (ABI 2.3).

### `node.raw.makeDir(path) -> boolean`

Arguments: `path: string`  
*Lua only.*

Creates the folder and every missing parent inside the resource's folder; true when it already exists. false, "outside" for an escaped path (ABI 2.3).

### `node.raw.removeFile(path, recursive?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `path: string`, `recursive?: boolean?`  
*Lua only.*

Removes a file, or a folder -- an empty one, or any one with recursive = true (false, "not empty" otherwise). Never the resource folder itself. false, "outside" / "not found" (ABI 2.3).

### `node.raw.renameFile(from, to) -> boolean`

Arguments: `from: string`, `to: string`  
*Lua only.*

Renames or moves inside the resource's folder; both paths are checked. An existing target FILE is replaced, atomically where the OS renames atomically -- write(tmp) + rename(tmp, target) is the atomic write. Missing parents of the target are created. false, "outside" / "not found" / "rename failed" (ABI 2.3).

### `node.raw.copyFile(from, to) -> boolean`

Arguments: `from: string`, `to: string`  
*Lua only.*

Copies a file to a path inside the resource's folder, replacing the target; missing parents are created. false, "outside" / "not a file" / "copy failed" (ABI 2.3).

### `node.raw.watchFile(path, fn) -> number?`

Arguments: `path: string`, `fn: function`  
*Lua only.*

Calls fn(path, "changed" | "removed") on the worker within about a second of the file changing, appearing or disappearing -- a poll of the modification time on the file-writer thread, no OS watcher, so it is the same everywhere and costs one stat per watched file per second. Returns a watch id, or nil, "outside" / "limit" (64 watches per resource). A resource's watches are removed with it at unload (ABI 2.3).

### `node.raw.unwatchFile(id) -> boolean`

Arguments: `id: number`  
*Lua only.*

Stops a watch of this resource's; false for an id that is not one (ABI 2.3).

## Persistent storage

A per-resource JSON key/value store (storage/&lt;resource&gt;.json). Any JSON-serialisable Lua value; every mutation is flushed atomically, so the store survives a restart or a crash.

### `node.raw.storageSet(key, value) -> boolean`

Arguments: `key: string`, `value: any`  
C: `storage_set`

Stores value under key in this resource's store (storage/&lt;resource&gt;.json). value is any JSON-serialisable Lua value -- string, number, boolean, table; a table with consecutive integer keys is an array. Every mutation is flushed atomically. Returns false when the key is empty or the value cannot be encoded.

### `node.raw.storageGet(key) -> record{a}?`

Arguments: `key: string`  
C: `storage_get`

Reads the value stored under key, decoded back into a Lua value; nil when the key is absent.

### `node.raw.storageDelete(key) -> boolean`

Arguments: `key: string`  
C: `storage_delete`

Removes key from this resource's store. Returns false when it was not there.

## Crypto and JSON

SHA-256, HMAC-SHA256, cryptographic random bytes, and JSON encode/decode with a depth guard.

### `node.raw.sha256(data) -> string`

Arguments: `data: string`  
C: `sha256_hex`

SHA-256 of data as a 64-character lower-case hex string.

### `node.raw.hmac(key, data) -> string`

Arguments: `key: string`, `data: string`  
C: `hmac_sha256_hex`

HMAC-SHA256 of data under key, as a 64-character lower-case hex string.

### `node.raw.randomBytes(count) -> string?`

Arguments: `count: number`  
C: `random_bytes`

count cryptographically random bytes (1..65536) as a binary string; nil outside that range. Use node.sha256 on the result for a printable token.

### `node.raw.jsonEncode(value, opts?) -> string`

Arguments: `value: any`, `opts?: any?`  
*Lua only.*

Encodes a Lua value as JSON. Tables with consecutive integer keys from 1 become arrays, other tables objects; functions and userdata are not encodable. opts (ABI 2.3): { pretty = true } indents by two spaces, { pretty = N } by N; without opts the compact form, byte for byte what it always was. Non-ASCII is never escaped to \uXXXX.

### `node.raw.jsonFlatten(value) -> table`

Arguments: `value: any`  
*Lua only.*

RFC 6901: the value as one flat table of JSON-pointer keys -- { ["/a/b/0"] = 1, ["/a/c"] = "x" }; a scalar becomes { [""] = value }. Keys escape ~ as ~0 and / as ~1. An EMPTY table has no leaves and is flattened to null, i.e. dropped -- it does not come back from unflatten (the RFC 6901 flat form cannot carry it). What node.storage and key-value tables want (ABI 2.3).

### `node.raw.jsonUnflatten(flat) -> any?`

Arguments: `flat: table`  
*Lua only.*

The inverse of jsonFlatten: a table of JSON-pointer keys back into the nested value. nil, err when a key is not a JSON pointer, the keys conflict (a scalar where an object is needed) or the result is deeper than the parser allows (ABI 2.3).

### `node.raw.jsonDiff(a, b) -> array<record{op,path,value?}>`

Arguments: `a: any`, `b: any`  
*Lua only.*

RFC 6902: the operations that turn a into b -- an array of { op = "add" | "remove" | "replace", path = <JSON pointer>, value = ... }; empty for equal values. jsonPatch(a, jsonDiff(a, b)) equals jsonDecode(jsonEncode(b)). Computed on the server, not in Lua (ABI 2.3).

### `node.raw.jsonPatch(base, ops) -> any?`

Arguments: `base: any`, `ops: any`  
*Lua only.*

RFC 6902: applies an array of operations (add, remove, replace, move, copy, test) to a COPY of base and returns the result; base is never modified. nil, err on a path that does not exist, a failed test, an operation that is not one, or a result deeper than the parser allows (ABI 2.3).

### `node.raw.jsonDecode(text) -> any?`

Arguments: `text: string`  
*Lua only.*

Decodes JSON text into a Lua value; nil on a parse error or when nesting exceeds the depth guard. JSON null becomes nil.

## Resources: lifecycle

Reloading a resource by name. The call returns `accepted`: the reload runs on the worker after the current handler returns, dropping the resource's registrations, coroutines and state, then loading it again.

### `node.raw.reloadResource(name) -> boolean`

Arguments: `name: string`  
C: `reload_resource`

Queues a reload of the named resource: its registrations, timers, coroutines and state are dropped and the resource is loaded again -- on the worker, after the current handler returns, so a resource can reload itself. Returns true when the request was ACCEPTED, not when the reload finished; a name that is not loaded returns false.

### `node.raw.getManifest() -> record{client,config,name,server,type,version}`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_resource_manifest_json`

This resource's manifest, resource.toml, as a table: { name, version, type, server = { main }, client = { files, obfuscation }, config = the [config] table as written (an empty table when there is none) }. The way to give a resource settings without a config.lua and a dofile: put them under [config] and read node.getManifest().config. Read from disk on each call. nil only when the manifest cannot be parsed.

## The join queue

A full server lets a signed-in player wait instead of refusing him ([queue] in server.toml). A waiting player is no player yet -- it has no player id -- so the queue names it by its directory account id. While it waits its launcher runs the install check, fetches the mod list and downloads what it lacks, at most [queue] downloads_while_waiting of them at once and after the downloads of joined players; a place that frees goes to the first in the queue whose mods are ready, and one still downloading keeps its place. The functions list the queue, move, remove and admit waiting accounts, pause it with a note the waiting players read, and change the [queue] limits; the events (queueJoinRequest, queueAdmitRequest, queueAdmitted, queueLeft, queueNoShow, playerJoinSlow) let a resource decide who waits where and who comes next, and follow what happens.

### `node.raw.getQueue() -> record{note,paused,waiting}`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_queue_json`

The join queue: { paused, note, waiting = { { accountId, name, roles, ip, installId, position, ready, admitWhenReady, waitedSec }, ... } }, waiting in queue order.

### `node.raw.queueMove(accountId, position) -> boolean`

Arguments: `accountId: number`, `position: number`  
C: `queue_move`

Moves a waiting account to position (1 = first). false when it is not waiting.

### `node.raw.queueRemove(accountId, reason?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `accountId: number`, `reason?: string?`  
C: `queue_remove`

Takes a waiting account out of the queue with a reason the player reads. false when it is not waiting.

### `node.raw.queueAdmit(accountId) -> boolean`

Arguments: `accountId: number`  
C: `queue_admit`

Lets a waiting account in past max_players once its mods are ready. false when it is not waiting.

### `node.raw.queuePause(paused, note?) -> boolean`

Arguments: `paused: number`, `note?: string?`  
C: `queue_pause`

Pauses the queue (1) with a note the waiting players read, or lets it go on (0).

### `node.raw.getQueueLimits() -> record{downloadsWhileWaiting,enabled,maxNoShows,maxWaiting,maxWaitingPerIp,noShowBarMin,noShowWindowMin}`

Arguments: none  
C: `get_queue_limits_json`

The [queue] settings: { enabled, maxWaiting, maxWaitingPerIp, downloadsWhileWaiting, maxNoShows, noShowWindowMin, noShowBarMin }.

### `node.raw.setQueueLimits(limits) -> boolean`

Arguments: `limits: any`  
C: `set_queue_limits_json`

Changes [queue] settings at runtime: a table with any of the keys limits() returns. false, and nothing changed, when a value is out of range.
